  • Emplifi's 2026 benchmark of 10,110 Facebook Reels from 704 brand pages found human speech in the first three seconds raised 10 second retention by nearly 25% and engagement by 5.6% against music only videos, so Bro Bros Shorts and Reels should open with a Bro speaking one short line ('Can you jump like me?') before or over the music. (Emplifi via The Reel Stars, Mar 2026, medium)
  • In the same Emplifi data a face in the first three seconds improved short view retention by about 10%, very short looping clips under 7 seconds got 18.7% more replays and 16.1% more engagement, and vertical video had 20.9% more reach than other shapes. (Emplifi via The Reel Stars, Mar 2026, medium)
  • Dash Social's Q2 2026 Shorts benchmarks (via eMarketer) show wellness Shorts with guided, follow along routines averaging 116% viewed (people replay them) against an 84.6% overall benchmark, the only category above 100% every quarter since Q1 2025, so follow along kids routines (brush teeth, stretch, copy the dance) are the Short type most likely to loop. (eMarketer, 2026, medium)
  • Instagram's 'Your Algorithm' topic controls spread from Reels (global English, Jan 2026) to Explore (15 Apr 2026) and the main Feed (Jun 2026), so parents can now add topics like 'kids songs' across the app, which makes clear topic captions and hashtags matter in every surface. (SocialPilot, Sep 2026, medium)
  • SocialPilot's tracker reported no significant Reels ranking changes in August or September 2026 and puts Explore's average reach rate at about 37.8%, the highest surface, against about 30.8% for Reels overall. (SocialPilot, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Planoly's October 2026 review found no independent large study of Trial Reels; Instagram's own claim (80% of frequent posters saw more non follower reach) is unverified and small tests swing from about 180 to 1.4M views, so Trial Reels are a cheap hook test, not a strategy. (Planoly, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Across about 800K videos from 71K accounts, Metricool found 2026 views up 76% but estimated ad revenue down 55%, with ad impressions down 51% and monetised playbacks down 59%, because viewers left long videos earlier (average view about 2.5 minutes, down from about 4). (Creator Handbook (Metricool study), Aug 2026, high)
  • The same study found the average Short now runs about 16 seconds and Shorts drive 61% of organic views, so Shorts bring reach while revenue still depends on keeping kids watching long compilations past the first minutes. (Creator Handbook (Metricool study), Aug 2026, medium)
  • Metricool's data showed two to four long form uploads a week performed best per video, suggesting Bro Bros should keep daily output in Shorts and Reels while making fewer, stronger long songs and compilations. (Creator Handbook (Metricool study), Aug 2026, medium)
  • Amazon Kids+ launched in Italy on 19 Oct 2026 for children aged 3 to 12 at 6.99 euros a month for Prime members (7.99 otherwise), ad free, with series, Kids+ Originals, full games without in app purchases and audiobooks, widening ad free subscription viewing in Europe. (SmartWorld, Oct 2026, high)
  • In mid November 2026 only one holiday song sat in the iTunes kids top 20, so on kids download charts the Christmas wave starts later than on Reels, leaving room to land a holiday song in late November. (PopVortex iTunes chart, Nov 2026, medium)
  • A brand new BabyBus song gains only about 900 views an hour in its first half day (46K in 13 hours) on a 44M subscriber channel, so even giant kids channels rely on slow building back catalogue views rather than launch day spikes. (vidIQ, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Instagram now shows each Reel with 100+ plays a second by second retention curve and a skip rate (viewers who swipe away before 3 seconds), filling in about 48 hours; one analysis of about 200 Reels rates skip under 20% and 3 second retention above 70% as strong, and skip above 40% as poor. (CreatorStack, Mar 2026, medium)
  • Socialinsider's study of about 25M brand Reels (2025 data, updated Nov 2026) finds Reels gather most views in the first 5 days, slow from day 5 to 10, fall sharply after day 10 and plateau around day 20, so judge a Reel after about a week and repost or remix winners later. (Socialinsider, Nov 2026, low)
  • A YouTube Short longer than one minute that carries an active Content ID claim can be blocked worldwide, so before any Bro Bros song is sent to a distributor that enrols it in Content ID, our own YouTube channel must be whitelisted, or our 60 to 90 second Shorts could vanish. (Bytecap research, Jul 2026, high)
  • In OpusClip's count of 2.9M YouTube clips made by its users over 12 months, 40% were 30 to 60 seconds, 28% were 15 to 30, 15% were 60 to 90, 12% were over 90 and only 4% were under 15 seconds; this shows what creators make, not what performs, so our own retention data should decide Short length. (Bytecap / OpusClip, Jul 2026, low)
  • Planoly's Oct 2026 rules for Trial Reels: use fresh footage (reused clips may be held back), change only one thing per test such as the hook, compare trials only with other trials using sends, likes and watch time, wait at least 24 hours, and judge patterns over several weeks, a cheap way to test two openings of the same song. (Planoly, Oct 2026, medium)
YouTube algorithm · 146
  • YouTube's recommender has two goals: help each viewer find what they want and maximise long-term satisfaction; in 2026 satisfaction (surveys, rewatches, 'not interested') outweighs raw watch time. (YouTube blog via SocialPilot / Shopify, 2026, high)
  • Recommendations are not one system: Home, Suggested, Search, Shorts feed and Subscriptions each rank with different signals; Suggested looks mainly at the video being watched now. (posteverywhere / subsub.io, Oct 2026, high)
  • Long-form: click-through rate on thumbnail+title and average view duration work as a pair; the thumbnail must deliver what it promises or satisfaction drops. (blckalpaca / vidIQ, 2026, high)
  • Shorts run on a separate engine: each Short goes to a seed audience (a few hundred viewers); if they watch instead of swiping away, reach widens in waves. (vidIQ / OutlierKit, 2026, high)
  • Shorts key signals: viewed vs swiped away, watch-through %, loops/replays and shares; benchmark about 65% average view duration for Shorts under 30 s, about 50% for 30-60 s. (socialync / dataslayer, 2026, medium)
  • The first 1.5 to 3 seconds of a Short decide most swipe-aways, so the opening frame must already be the hook. (easyviral / clipspeed, 2026, medium)
  • Since 1 Oct 2026 YouTube's recommender gives more reach to original Shorts; reused or inauthentic content is reviewed at channel level. (AIR Media-Tech (YouTube partner), Oct 2026, high)
  • Views now count from the first frame (Shorts since Mar 2025, all formats since Aug 2026), so raw views look bigger; engaged views and retention are the real health metrics. (SocialPilot, Sep 2026, high)
  • Made-for-kids videos lose comments, notification bell, cards/end screens and personalised ads, so discovery relies even more on watch behaviour, Search and Suggested. (AIR glossary / YouTube Help, high)
  • YouTube Kids app recommendations come from what each child profile watched and searched; homepage shelves are series, music, learning, discovery and games. (YouTube Kids Help / ppc.land, high)
  • New channels typically get cautious distribution for the first few months until the system has performance history; consistency builds that trust. (FluxNote, Mar 2026, medium)
  • Instagram Reels rank on watch time first, then sends (DM shares) per reach, then likes per reach; sends matter most for reaching non-followers; reposts and watermarks are demoted. (SocialPilot Aug 2026 / Later, high)
  • For kids songs, compilations and 'Up Next' sessions matter: if the next suggested video is yours, you keep the session; stitch 5-6 songs into mega mixes led by the best performer. (nursery rhyme growth analyses, medium)
  • Because made for kids videos lose end screens, cards and the bell, keyword named playlists are the main discovery tool; descriptions should link to the first video of a related playlist so autoplay continues in order. (AIR Media-Tech, Oct 2026, medium)
  • For kids and teens long form, Friday is the best upload day and Wednesday the weakest, a +379% gap (the biggest of any niche); peak slots are 16:00 UTC and 23:00 UTC, and 10 to 20 minute videos fit best. (AIR study of 31,852 videos, Jul 2026, medium)
  • 24/7 live streams of the back catalogue gave a kids channel 41.9% of its watch time from under a fifth of its views, at 66% higher RPM; big kids channels run 4 to 5 streams at once like mini TV stations. (AIR case, Mar 2026, medium)
  • A kids stream view earned about 1.45 USD per 1,000 versus 0.43 for a normal video view; on one 8M channel streams were only 4% of views while total channel views grew 53%. (AIR, Oct 2026, medium)
  • In a 282 channel study, large kids channels earned the most total revenue at 16 or more videos a month, so our 14 a week cadence is in the winning range. (AIR, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Once a channel is in the YouTube Kids app it can become the main source: YouTube Kids brought 67% of traffic for Baby Ben en Espanol and 68 to 78% for the BIBO toy channels. (AIR, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Since Jan 2026 parents can cap the Shorts feed for kids and teens, even to zero, so Shorts cannot be the only growth engine; long form and playlists must carry the channel. (AIR / YouTube, Oct 2026, medium)
  • YouTube keeps autoplay off by default and Take a Break and Bedtime reminders on for signed in under 18s, so continuation to our next video cannot be assumed; long compilations and playlists inside one session matter more. (YouTube blog, Jan 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's Todd Beaupre (Sep 2026): a new upload's first hour is not a subscriber test; it is shown to mixed audiences including people who never watched the channel, and each audience is judged separately. (Creator Insider via PPC Land, Sep 2026, high)
  • Even a channel's best videos are usually picked by only about 10% or fewer of subscribers who see them in the feed, so low subscriber click rate is normal and not a penalty. (Creator Insider via PPC Land, Sep 2026, high)
  • Recommendations are built fresh per viewer at the start of each session; no single metric decides, and the weight of clicks, watch time, surveys and likes changes by person, device and moment. (Creator Insider via PPC Land, Sep 2026, high)
  • In the YouTube Kids app, new child profiles get no personal recommendations until they build history, so they browse curated shelves (series, music, learning, discovery, games); being in the music and learning shelves is key for a new song channel. (Team YouTube FAQ via PPC Land, Apr 2026, high)
  • YouTube's Shorts lead says there is no ideal Short length, posting time or daily quota and hashtags are optional; a Short that stalls needs a fresh new Short, because deleting and reposting can be read as spam. (Creator Insider via OutlierKit, Oct 2026, high)
  • WOA Song Spanish (kids songs) deleted long videos that skirted kids policies and posted 31 quality checked Shorts in Q4 2025 (up from 8); views jumped from 27M to 1.46B in one quarter, with Shorts giving over 98% of views. (AIR Media-Tech case, May 2026, medium)
  • Weak or policy risky old videos can hold a kids channel back; cleaning the library and applying kids quality rules (educational value, positive role models, clear visuals, correct metadata) preceded the breakout. (AIR Media-Tech case, May 2026, medium)
  • A kids channel that cut long uploads from 21 to 11 a quarter, doubled Shorts cut from its funniest long form moments and moved to 4K TV friendly picture and sound grew revenue 34% while views grew only 5%. (AIR Media-Tech case, Apr 2026, medium)
  • Kids channels see a holiday view spike in late November and early December, so the next three weeks are the time to post more Shorts. (AIR Media-Tech cases, 2026, medium)
  • In Sept 2026 YouTube's inauthentic content enforcement suspended channels of long sleep music and prayer compilations built on one template; our lullaby and mega mix compilations must vary songs, visuals and order, not repeat one template. (AIR Media-Tech, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Since 22 Oct 2026 US viewers can save up to 3 custom Home feeds and the mobile Home top bar has separate Live, Music and Movies and TV feeds, with Subscriptions moved there; clear music metadata helps songs land in the Music feed. (AIR Media-Tech, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Ask YouTube (conversational answers on web, mobile and TV in the US since 12 Oct 2026) can answer a query without a click, so titles and descriptions should match the exact questions parents ask (for example a song to help a toddler brush teeth). (AIR Media-Tech, Nov 2026, low)
  • Creator analysis since late 2025 says the recommender ranks by expected satisfied watch time per click rather than watch time per impression, and older Shorts that ran for years lost distribution, so a steady flow of fresh Shorts beats relying on the back catalogue. (PPC Land, Dec 2025, medium)
  • Creator data from over 1,000 channels suggests Home now shows as few as 2 long form videos per row with reserved Shorts slots (down from about 6), so long form songs depend more on Suggested, Search and playlists than on Home. (PPC Land, Dec 2025, low)
  • At Made on YouTube (23 Sep 2026) YouTube announced video A/B testing, AI feedback on drafts in Ask Studio, Shorts series and a live auto dubbing pilot for early 2027. (AIR Media-Tech, Nov 2026, medium)
  • The Melobies, an energetic nursery rhyme channel, grew subscribers about 9 times (917%) after a package of fixes: listing in YouTube Kids, more expressive characters with high contrast simple mobile thumbnails, keyword matched titles, playlists grouped by theme and 24/7 best of streams; its top video then hit 3.5M views, triple its norm. (AIR Media-Tech, Sep 2025, medium)
  • An audit of The Melobies flagged poorly organised compilations as a watch time leak; compilations should open with the strongest song and keep one theme so viewers stay through. (AIR Media-Tech, Sep 2025, medium)
  • Sandy the Sticker (near 1M subs) went from 1 manual to 16 automated 24/7 streams, added Shorts cut from high energy long form moments and raised long uploads 44% in a quarter: watch time rose 31%, average view duration 1:36 to 2:06, revenue 63%. (AIR Media-Tech, Mar 2026, medium)
  • Across these kids cases, 24/7 live streams of the back catalogue are repeatedly named the main growth driver once a channel has a few dozen strong songs, so a looping stream is worth starting after we pass about 50 songs. (AIR Media-Tech cases 2025 to 2026, low)
  • YouTube says (Jan 2026) its recommender automatically breaks repeated viewing loops in sensitive categories for young viewers, and YouTube Kids, for ages 0 to 12, is a curated walled garden shaped with child development specialists to favour content that sparks curiosity and positive behaviour. (YouTube Official Blog, Jan 2026, high)
  • Creators report YouTube now filters sensational or clickbait titles: one video with a 9% click rate stalled after its title was flagged, so calm, honest kids titles protect reach. (Marketing School, Feb 2026, low)
  • Buffer's study of 1.8M videos (Jul 2026) found long form engagement peaks on Sunday mornings in viewers' local time (10 AM best, 8 to 11 AM strong) and is weakest on Wednesday and Thursday afternoons. (Buffer, Jul 2026, high)
  • In the same study Shorts engagement peaks on Friday from 4 to 7 PM local time and in evenings (6 to 11 PM), with Monday and Tuesday weakest, so our 4 PM IST Shorts land in the evening slot for India and the Gulf but at dawn in the Americas. (Buffer, Jul 2026, medium)
  • For living room kids viewing, AIR advises 20 to 30 minute themed compilations, mini arcs of 3 to 4 linked episodes, a hook in the first seconds because autoplay gives little time, 4K upload and thumbnails with one bold central element readable from across a room. (AIR Media-Tech, Oct 2024, medium)
  • Ending compilations with a bonus clip (a blooper or behind the scenes dance) keeps kids watching to the end and lifts session watch time. (AIR Media-Tech, Oct 2024, low)
  • Suggested videos are chosen from the video being watched plus co watch patterns (viewers of channel A also watch channel B) and satisfaction, not keywords alone, so each song should be a natural next watch after a big channel's popular song on the same topic. (vidIQ, Jul 2026, high)
  • YouTube Studio's 'Channels your audience watches' and 'What your audience watches' reports show which channels and topics to sit beside; checking them monthly tells us which competitors' hits to answer with our own songs. (vidIQ, Jul 2026, medium)
  • Shorts whose pace or topic differ from a channel's long videos can attract viewers who never watch the long form, which can lower long form recommendations; Shorts should be micro versions of the full song that point to it. (Gyre, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Warning signs that Shorts are hurting long form: falling long form click rate, weak Shorts feed traffic, retention dropping sharply in the first 30 seconds and fewer returning viewers; test a month of song bridge Shorts before deciding on a separate Shorts channel. (Gyre, Nov 2026, low)
  • Metricool's 2026 US study (799,718 videos, 71,177 accounts): long form views rose 76% year on year but watch time only 11%, engagement fell 45% and ad impressions 51%, as viewers leave long videos sooner; Shorts are now 61% of measured views. (Tubefilter, Aug 2026, high)
  • The same study found 2 to 4 long form videos a week is the sweet spot for long form views per channel; above that, extra uploads add little, which supports fewer, stronger long songs plus compilations. (Tubefilter, Aug 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's Shorts ranking leans on swipe through rate, loop rate and first second engagement, and from May 2026 viewer satisfaction (surveys, return visits) ranks above raw watch time for long form. (MilX, Jul 2026, medium)
  • Click rate benchmarks cited for 2026: 4 to 8% is normal, 10% or more excellent and under 2% a red flag; we can track this per song in YouTube Studio. (MilX, Jul 2026, low)
  • In the same network the Shorts feed went from 6.6% to 81.8% of traffic and Search fell from 31.9% to 6.5%, and revenue rose 386% against 1140% for views: a Shorts surge brings reach far faster than money, so long songs and compilations still carry earnings. (air.io, Apr 2026, medium)
  • The network's Q4 playbook was a full quality audit, a review of every upload as it went live, more posting ahead of the New Year holidays and a harder Shorts push on every channel; late November to early January is the window to raise output. (air.io, Apr 2026, medium)
  • Azooz and Jude (kids characters, 265K subs) grew views 51% and revenue 140% in Q4 2025 by posting on a predictable schedule with no gaps, upgrading to 4K, using one consistent structure for titles, descriptions and tags, and adding compilations to lengthen sessions. (air.io, Feb 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's Shorts product lead describes Shorts ranking as finding a seed audience that may enjoy a Short and then widening or stopping reach by their response ('the audience is the algorithm'); early views of a new creator are exploratory, so a flop is a lesson, not a penalty. (outlierkit.com (Creator Insider), 2026, medium)
  • Reels analysis (Jun 2026) says Instagram scores connected (follower) and unconnected reach separately, watch completion is the dominant signal, saves and shares outweigh likes, and Reels under 15 seconds rarely win non follower reach, so our Reels should be 20 to 40 second chorus cuts. (virlo.ai, Jun 2026, medium)
  • On a 3M subscriber kids channel (Q4 2025) joining 2 to 3 minute songs into roughly 10 minute compilations raised compilation watch duration 19%, CPM 9% and RPM 72%; with several Shorts a day total views rose 27% and revenue 16%. (AIR, 2026, high)
  • The same channel cut duplicate risk by giving every re upload or compilation its own visuals and never repeating identical sequences, which matters as YouTube reviews reused content at channel level. (AIR, 2026, medium)
  • Vania Mania's Indonesian channel posted fewer videos but cleared copyright claims on old uploads, and its Suggested views jumped from 32K to 3.75M in a quarter: claimed videos are held back from recommendations. (AIR, 2026, high)
  • Vania Mania Kids Live raised pre holiday uploads from 32 to 42 and fixed packaging, and views grew from 3.1M to 11.4M with Suggested traffic up 351%, another sign that late Q4 is the moment to post more. (AIR, 2026, high)
  • A platform bug once removed Vania Mania's multi language audio tracks; recovering them plus metadata fixes lifted the French channel's views 37% and revenue 60%, so check our dubbed audio tracks are still attached every month. (AIR, 2026, medium)
  • Since 8 Nov 2026 US viewers can create Home feeds from typed prompts (for example 'calm songs for my toddler'), and these custom feeds now sit in the top bar and replace the standard topic chips, so clear plain words in titles and descriptions about age, mood and topic help a song match those prompts. (Social Media Today, Nov 2026, high)
  • On 6 Nov 2026 YouTube expanded Hype with Jewels to the US, Canada, the UK, Germany, Ireland, Italy, Poland, Spain and Turkey for Partner Program channels with 500 to 500,000 subscribers, letting viewers and creators buy reach boosts; it matters only once we join the Partner Program. (Social Media Today, Nov 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's 1 Oct 2026 Shorts originality post says minor technical edits, template based bulk changes and narration over clips do not make content original; it does not cover cutting your own long videos, so each Bro Bros Short should get its own vertical framing, hook line or ending rather than a plain trim. (PPC Land, Oct 2026, medium)
  • A 5.68M subscriber kids channel cut long uploads by 35% (82 to 53 a quarter), dropped weak formats, redesigned thumbnails and titles to fit kids policies, posted more Shorts and more before Christmas: in Q4 2025 views rose 76%, revenue 153%, Suggested traffic 84% and Shorts feed traffic 348%. (AIR, 2026, high)
  • Emir Ela, a kids channel, grew from 16.5M to 475M quarterly views (Q4 2025 against Q3) after clearer visuals, better pacing and sound design, new thumbnails and a balance of learning and fun across Shorts, long videos and streams; long form views rose the most. (AIR, 2026, medium)
  • AIR's kids timing guide: post 2 to 3 hours before the audience's peak in YouTube Studio's 'When your viewers are on YouTube' chart, put compilations in mid morning or early evening TV time, give a series a fixed weekly slot, and without data test weekend 8 to 11 AM US Eastern. (AIR, 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's 'valued watch time' counts only time on videos viewers rate four or five stars in satisfaction surveys, and a model predicts that rating for viewers who never answer, so a song that kids rewatch but parents rate poorly can still lose reach; making songs parents enjoy too protects it. (OutlierKit (YouTube blog 2021, Creator Insider Sep 2026), high)
  • YouTube Studio shows no survey or satisfaction score, so the practical proxies are the retention graph, likes, and how each song compares with our own other songs over the same days. (OutlierKit, Oct 2026, medium)
  • YouTube Studio's Inspiration tab (English only, desktop) has been phased out since Aug 2026, and Ask Studio is now the built in brainstorming and analytics assistant, using our audience's last 28 days of viewing to suggest topics. (YouTube Help, 2026, medium)
  • AIR's length study (18,080 channels, 4.5M videos from 2025 to Mar 2026) found video length makes no measurable difference to engagement for kids content at scale, so length should follow the viewing moment (short songs, 10 minute mixes, long TV compilations), not a magic number. (AIR, Jun 2026, medium)
  • In one kids case, moving from 28+ minute compilations to shorter formats raised average percentage viewed from 8 to 12% to 16 to 22% and lifted recommendation traffic, so very long compilations should be a weekly extra, not the main upload. (AIR, Jun 2026, medium)
  • On one kids channel dubbed audio tracks added 125.5M views in five months (over 30% of its total), and a Portuguese track on three channels brought almost 5M extra views in six months, the cheapest growth lever in AIR's cases. (AIR, 2026, high)
  • DONA English's new 24/7 streams earned 20% of the channel's revenue in their first 10 days and 60% the following month, because kids stream views paid a median 1.45 USD per 1,000 against 0.43 for normal kids video views. (AIR, 2026, medium)
  • Todd Beaupre (Creator Insider, 1 Sep 2026): Home does not try to find a single category for a channel; it ranks each video for each viewer, and many viewers watch hundreds of unrelated channels, so our mixed song topics are not penalised as long as each video satisfies its own viewers. (OutlierKit (Creator Insider), Sep 2026, high)
  • Beaupre also said Home weighs how a video performs in the Home context, not through embeds, ads or shared links, and that wider reach brings broader audiences and naturally lower click rates, so a falling click rate while impressions grow is normal. (OutlierKit (Creator Insider), Sep 2026, high)
  • Widely shared claims of a February 2026 'micro niche cluster' overhaul of Home had no YouTube source and were withdrawn by the blogs that repeated them; treat algorithm rumours without an official YouTube statement as unproven. (OutlierKit, Oct 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's Test and Compare (global since Dec 2025) tests up to three titles, thumbnails or pairs for up to two weeks and picks the winner by watch time per impression, not click rate, so a title that wins clicks but loses viewers will not win. (searchenginejournal.com, Dec 2025, high)
  • An Arabic language kids channel translated the titles and descriptions of its whole library into 11 languages (English, German, French, Spanish, Portuguese, Dutch, Japanese, Korean, Polish, Italian, Turkish); within about six months views from those markets rose 77%, subscribers 58%, and viewers arrived from 11 new countries led by the US, Brazil, Germany and Turkey. (air.io, May 2025, medium)
  • A 7M subscriber Arabic kids channel that had lost 91% of its recommendations replaced the thumbnails across its whole catalogue and then gained 2.8 times the views, about 5 times the recommendations and 6.6 times the revenue: thumbnails act as trust signals for the whole channel, not just one video. (air.io, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Kids channel Emir Ela grew from 16.5M to 475M views in one quarter (Q3 to Q4 2025) after adding trends gradually, improving visual clarity, pacing and sound design, balancing learning with fun and publishing on a set plan across Shorts, long videos and streams; long form views rose about 165 times. (air.io, Feb 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's Player for Education (Jul 2026) is an ad free embedded player with no sidebar, recommendations or comments; by default K to 12 students on school accounts and school Chromebooks can watch only videos a teacher assigns, and licensing fees go to creators, so in schools teachers, not the algorithm, choose which songs children see. (air.io, Jul 2026, high)
  • YouTube said at Made on YouTube (Sep 2026) that over 140M people used Ask YouTube in June; a custom Gemini model matches each question to the moment in a video that answers it, and clear timestamp chapters plus a detailed description are the signals YouTube itself recommends. (uppbeat.io, Oct 2026, high)
  • YouTube's Gemini search reads speech, captions, on screen text, visuals and chapters as answer fragments, so in our songs the key topic words should be sung clearly (brush your teeth, wash your hands), shown as on screen labels and spelled right in captions, and each chapter of a compilation should make sense on its own. (webiano.digital, Jun 2026, medium)
  • Ask YouTube previews appear mainly for open ended, how to and skill questions, which for families means routine help ('song to help toddler sleep', 'learn colours song'), so routine and learning songs are the ones AI search can surface. (uppbeat.io, Oct 2026, medium)
  • vidIQ's study of 448,413 channels started in Jan to Jun 2025 (Jul 2026) found a new channel's median first long video gets about 180 views in 30 days and its tenth about 272 (+51%), while Shorts stay flat (about 1,360 first, 1,344 tenth): long form builds with each upload, Shorts do not. (vidiq.com, Jul 2026, high)
  • In the same study the best of a new channel's first ten videos got about 6.5 times the first for Shorts (8,832 views) and about 11 times for long form (1,948), and only 10% of long videos and 21% of Shorts passed 10,000 views; vidIQ's conclusion is that upload volume mainly buys more tries at the one video that breaks out. (vidiq.com, Jul 2026, high)
  • Because viewers cannot save made for kids songs to their own playlists or Watch Later, the only way to keep a family coming back to a set of Bro Bros songs is our own channel playlists, compilations and live streams, plus a memorable channel name parents can search. (support.google.com, 2026, medium)
  • Todd Beaupre (Creator Insider, Sep 2026) said the watch page Suggested list, not the Subscriptions feed, is the top source of views even from a channel's own subscribers, so each Bro Bros song must sit well next to similar kids songs to be found again. (ppc.land, Sep 2026, high)
  • YouTube tested capping how often one video is recommended to the same viewer at about eight times after finding a viewer shown the same uploads 19 times without clicking, so an ignored song will stop being offered and a fresh thumbnail or a new song is the way back. (ppc.land, Sep 2026, medium)
  • In YouTube Kids a parent's block hides a video only on that child's profile, while a report goes to a 24/7 review queue and, if upheld, removes the video for every user, so one misleading thumbnail can cost a song the whole app. (ppc.land (Team YouTube FAQ), Apr 2026, high)
  • In the same study channels posting about 5 Shorts a month averaged 8,789 views per long video against 4,364 for channels with no Shorts, but the lift disappeared at 35 or more Shorts a month, a hint (correlation only) that each Short should point clearly to its full song rather than flooding the feed. (metricool.com, Jul 2026, medium)
  • Video length beat every thumbnail factor in Eden's data: 41.1% of videos over 40 minutes broke out against 26.9% under 5 minutes, which supports pairing each 2 to 3 minute song with long themed compilations of 30 minutes or more. (eden.so, Aug 2026, medium)
  • A 3M+ subscriber kids channel (Q3 to Q4 2025) gave its near identical uploads, such as a normal and an acoustic version of one song, clearly different visuals with no shared sequences to avoid duplicate content risk, alongside the compilation change that lifted its views 27%. (air.io, Mar 2026, high)
  • Another kids channel replaced exaggerated or negative emotion thumbnails with calm, positive, colourful and accurate ones and fixed technical issues through a YouTube review: Browse traffic grew from 6.4M to 17.7M views a quarter (+173%), Suggested from 58M to 75M, total views +41% and revenue +59%. (air.io, Apr 2026, high)
  • In the same data a wholesome rural creator's Shorts average about 71,000 views while her long video median is 840, which the analysts read as audiences snacking on the Shorts feed rather than following a person, so Shorts views alone will not build a song channel's loyal audience without long songs and playlists. (creatordb.app, Sep 2026, medium)
  • YouTube Kids search results are chosen by algorithm without human review and can include recommendations from the child's past viewing; a parent can switch search off, which limits that child to channels verified by YouTube Kids, so being a verified, trusted channel matters for families who lock search. (support.google.com (YouTube Kids Help), 2026, high)
  • Children can search YouTube Kids by voice on phones and on TVs, so song titles should start with words a 3 to 6 year old can say clearly ('Dino Stomp', 'Bath Bubbles') rather than invented spellings or long phrases. (support.google.com (YouTube Kids Help), 2026, medium)
  • Spotter's analysis found Related Shorts links lift long video views by 10% or more, up to 20% when the Short's title and theme closely match the linked video, and Shorts posted within 3 days of the long upload drive the most, since Shorts get most views in their first 7 days. (spottercommunity.beehiiv.com, Apr 2024, medium)
  • Because weekend activity in Metricool's model is about half of weekdays on both YouTube and Instagram, our weekly 25 to 45 minute compilation is best released on a weekday (Friday 10 AM fits) and weekends kept for the strongest single songs rather than tests. (Metricool best time tool, Nov 2026, low)
  • YouTube said on 1 Oct 2026 it is cutting Shorts feed reach for channels that mainly re upload content without adding anything of their own, judged at channel level, and that minor technical or template based edits do not make a clip original, so every Bro Bros Short must be a fresh cut with its own hook, not a resized copy. (ppc.land / YouTube community post, Oct 2026, high)
  • Todd Beaupre (Creator Insider, Sep 2026) said subscribers click about 10% or less of the uploads shown to them, one viewer was shown a creator's uploads 19 times without noticing, and YouTube has tested capping a video at about eight impressions per viewer, so with 14 songs a week most subscribers miss most songs and each title and thumbnail must win on its own. (ppc.land, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Beaupre also advised not benchmarking every upload against the channel's best hit and noted some evergreen videos gain most of their audience a month or more after publishing, so a kids song should be judged on 28 day data, not its first two days. (ppc.land, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Creator data gathered by Joos (Dec 2025, unverified) suggests the Home feed now shows as few as two long form videos per row with the next row mostly Shorts, roughly two long to five Shorts on desktop, cutting long form Home slots by up to 80% while Shorts views from Browse rose across 1,000+ channels. (ppc.land, Dec 2025, low)
  • Google said at Made on YouTube (23 Sep 2026) that over 300M users engage with text Posts daily and the Shorts feed will begin recommending Posts, but made for kids channels like ours cannot post them, so this new path helps family vlog rivals, not us. (9to5google.com, Sep 2026, medium)
  • In AIR's study of 18,000 English channels (Mar 2025 to Mar 2026), small kids channels with 100K to 1M monthly views that post only long form had about 63% more median subscribers (60,100 vs 36,750) than those mixing in Shorts, while at 10M+ monthly views the two groups were equal, so for a young kids channel long songs must stay the core. (air.io, May 2026, medium)
  • The same AIR study puts the best mix at about 25 to 40% Shorts by upload count (kids channels that mix average about 37%), says long form performance falls once Shorts pass about 55% of uploads, and advises shifting the mix gradually over two to three quarters. (air.io, May 2026, medium)
  • Since YouTube's Q1 2026 TV update (14 May 2026) supervised child and teen profiles appear on the TV 'Who's Watching' screen, so more kids watch on the living room TV inside their own supervised profile, where recommendations follow that child's history rather than the parents'. (ppc.land, May 2026, medium)
  • The same TV update added 'Up Next' teasers at the end of videos to ease the move into the next one, so the last seconds of a Bro Bros song should tease another Bro Bros song (a brother waving 'next, the Wiggle Bus!') rather than end on a blank card, since end screens are off for made for kids videos. (ppc.land, May 2026, medium)
  • Todd Beaupre (YouTube growth and discovery, Sep 2026) says the Suggested list beside and after a video is the biggest source of views even from a channel's own subscribers, far ahead of the subscriptions feed, so each Bro Bros song should end in a way that flows into our next song and sit in themed playlists. (Creator Insider via PPC Land, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Beaupre also says a brand new channel with no viewer history reaches people mainly through topic matching, so while we are small each song's title, description, first lines and visuals should all state the same single clear topic (brushing teeth, snow day) that families search and watch. (Creator Insider via PPC Land, Sep 2026, medium)
  • The YouTube Kids app has a Your Stuff tab with a Watch it Again row (plus shared and downloaded videos) and topic categories like learning and music on Home, so songs a child replays keep coming back to the top of that child's app; replay value is a direct discovery lever on YouTube Kids. (The Tech Outlook, Dec 2024, medium)
  • A 15.8M subscriber kids channel (Heidi dan Zidane) running four to five 24/7 streams of its old hits got 41.9% of its watch time (11.6M hours) and 34.5% of partner revenue from streams, with under one fifth of views, so once we have 30+ songs a nonstop Bro Bros stream is the cheapest way to turn the back catalogue into watch time. (AIR Media-Tech, Mar 2026 (vendor case study), medium)
  • In the same case, stream revenue per view was about 66% higher than regular uploads and streams stay on the YouTube Live shelf, so several themed streams (bedtime, dance party, learning) running at once act like small TV channels for different moods and time zones. (AIR Media-Tech, Mar 2026 (vendor case study), medium)
  • Since August 2026 long YouTube videos can carry localised thumbnails that switch to the viewer's language setting, so a Bro Bros song can show 'Snow Dance!' to English viewers and the same picture with Spanish or Portuguese words to those viewers without new uploads. (AIR, Nov 2026, medium)
  • AIR's month by month log of YouTube changes (updated 4 Nov 2026) lists no new ranking change in November so far; the last feed changes were the Shorts originality cut on 1 Oct and custom Home feeds on 22 Oct, so our strategy does not need to change this week. (AIR, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Little Dot Studios (800+ channels, 11.2B monthly views) found that in 2023 and 2024 videos over two hours took a huge share of watch time and revenue, with a 120 minute video earning on average about 100 times a 20 minute one, partly from Premium viewers who avoid ad breaks. (Tubefilter (Little Dot whitepaper), Feb 2026, high)
  • After a significant YouTube algorithm update in January 2025, Little Dot saw ultra long (2 hour plus) viewing fall more than 90% from its 2024 average within three months and the average length of recommended videos halve from about 80 to 40 minutes. (Tubefilter (Little Dot whitepaper), Feb 2026, high)
  • By 2025 Little Dot found average views per video had levelled out across short, long and ultra long formats, and the most popular videos' average length fell 21% (about 35 to 28 minutes) from Dec 2024 to May 2025, so 20 to 40 minute compilations match what YouTube now recommends. (Tubefilter (Little Dot whitepaper), Feb 2026, high)
  • YouTube's video A/B test (shown 23 to 25 Sep 2026) lets a creator upload up to three cuts of the same video, for example different first seconds, and compare their retention curves; unlike title and thumbnail tests it appears the creator, not the system, picks the winner. (PPC Land, Sep 2026, medium)
  • No launch date was given for video cut tests, but title testing went live 84 days after its 2025 announcement, which points to roughly mid December 2026 if YouTube follows the same pace; eligibility and made for kids access are not confirmed, and made for kids channels already lose some Studio tools. (PPC Land, Sep 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's coming 'dynamic thumbnails' will keep up to three creator chosen thumbnails live at once and show each to the viewer groups it suits best, instead of crowning one winner, which could let one Bro Bros song show a baby friendly image to some viewers and a dance image to others. (PPC Land, Sep 2026, medium)
  • The YouTube Kids home organises videos into five curated shelves (series, music, learning, discovery, games), and new users get no personalised recommendations until they have viewing history, so a Bro Bros song should clearly signal music plus one learning point in title and description to land on two shelves. (YouTube Help FAQ via PPC Land, Apr 2026, medium)
  • In YouTube Kids, recommendations are fully automated, an upheld parent report removes a video for all users, and filters are updated quickly when an unsuitable trend appears on main YouTube, so Bro Bros trend videos must not carry trend words or looks that filters may block (zombie, scary, prank) in titles or thumbnails. (YouTube Help FAQ via PPC Land, Apr 2026, medium)
  • Todd Beaupre (Creator Insider, 1 Sep 2026) said some videos peak on their first day while others keep finding audiences for a month or more, so a song with a slow start should be left alone for several weeks rather than deleted or re uploaded. (PPC Land, Sep 2026, medium)
  • AIR's month by month tracker of 2026 YouTube changes lists no kids specific ranking change in September or October 2026; the confirmed changes that touch us are Shorts originality (1 Oct), custom Home feeds (22 Oct) and parents' Shorts timers (Jan and Apr 2026). (AIR Media-Tech, Nov 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's 1 Oct 2026 originality guidance for Shorts says minor technical edits, templates or a voice over that just describes the on screen action do not count as adding value; original storytelling, commentary or distinctive editing does, so Bro Bros compilations and recut Shorts should each add a new hook, scene or caption idea. (PPC Land, Oct 2026, high)
  • Channels downranked in the Shorts feed for mostly reposting get no email, strike or status screen, so a sudden fall in Shorts views is the only sign; keeping every Short visibly new protects the whole channel. (PPC Land, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Meta reported that 75% of US Instagram recommendations came from original posts in Q4 2025, a sign that both apps now reward the account that first made a clip over reposters. (PPC Land, Oct 2026, medium)
  • The UK government consulted until 31 Aug 2026 on rules that could make YouTube promote public service broadcaster channels (BBC, ITV and others) more visibly, which, if adopted, could add more CBeebies style competition in UK recommendations. (Android Authority, Jul 2026, low)
  • In AIR's study of 31,852 long videos (2025 uploads, 301 channels), Kids and Teens was the most timing sensitive niche: Friday uploads had the best median views (about 36K) and Wednesday the worst, a 379% gap, with the strongest hour at 16:00 UTC and a second peak at 23:00 UTC. (AIR Media-Tech, Jul 2026, medium)
  • 16:00 UTC is 21:30 IST, while Bro Bros posts at 10:00 IST (04:30 UTC) and 16:00 IST (10:30 UTC), times that suit India and Europe but fall before most American families wake, so testing the weekly long compilation at about 21:30 IST on Fridays is worth one experiment. (AIR Media-Tech, Jul 2026, medium)
  • The same study found 10 to 20 minutes the ideal length band for kids and teens long form and weekdays about 8% better than weekends, while music single tracks of 3 to 10 minutes held about 50% retention. (AIR Media-Tech, Jul 2026, medium)
  • Metricool's 2026 study found 83% of a video's interactions arrive in its first 10 days and only 11% of channels under 10K subscribers moved up a size tier in a year, so early packaging matters and growth for a new kids channel is normally gradual. (Metricool, 2026, medium)
  • Ask YouTube, the conversational AI search launched in 2026, is open only to signed in US users aged 13 and over and excludes supervised accounts and signed out viewers (confirmed 6 Jul 2026); YouTube says classic search stays, so for kids content keyword titles, descriptions and chapters remain the way searches find us. (PPC Land, Jul 2026, high)
  • YouTube's discovery lead said (Sep 2026) the team tested capping how many times one video is recommended to the same viewer at about eight, after finding an upload recommended 19 times to someone who never noticed it, so a thumbnail and title that fail to earn a click keep wasting impressions and should be refreshed. (Creator Insider via PPC Land, Sep 2026, medium)
  • YouTube advises creators not to judge new uploads against their single best video, because new content can take time to find its audience and each viewer's ranking differs; for us that means judging each song after two to four weeks, not on day one. (Creator Insider via PPC Land, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Since 22 Oct 2026 the US mobile app has a four item bottom bar (Home, Shorts, Create, You) and a 'Subs' tab in the Home top bar beside Live, Music and custom feeds; custom feeds work only for signed in English speakers in the US with history on, so most families abroad still see the classic Home. (OutlierKit, Oct 2026, medium)
  • No Instagram Reels ranking change was confirmed between August and mid November 2026; the order remains watch time and completion first, then sends per reach, then likes per reach, so our Reels should keep optimising for full watches and shares between parents. (SocialPilot, Sep to Nov 2026, medium)
  • YouTube said on 1 Oct 2026 ('Prioritizing original content on Shorts') that the Shorts feed will give original videos more priority and reduce distribution for channels that mainly re upload others' clips without adding anything; it is judged for the whole channel with no notice, so Bro Bros must never pad the Shorts shelf with other creators' clips, and our own originals gain. (PPC Land, Oct 2026, high)
  • Since April 2026 viewers can set their daily Shorts limit to 0 minutes, which removes the Shorts tab, the Home Shorts shelf and Shorts in Search and Suggested on all their devices; some Shorts only channels saw a 7 to 14% impressions dip in the first week, so our long songs and compilations protect reach among parents who switch Shorts off. (OutlierKit, Apr 2026, medium)
  • Todd Beaupre (Creator Insider, Sep 2026) said early performance is learned separately for subscribers and for new viewers, so a video can do poorly with subscribers yet still spread to new audiences, most viewing of subscribed channels happens in the Suggested list rather than the Subscriptions feed, and a video should not be judged in its first hour because some peak much later. (PPC Land, Sep 2026, high)
  • Beaupre also advised using separate channels when audiences expect different things and not treating a channel's best video as the benchmark, which supports keeping Hindi stories and English songs on separate Bro Bros channels and judging each song against our median, not our top hit. (PPC Land, Sep 2026, medium)

  • Nine of those top 15 are animated song or rhyme brands, and the shared traits are simple repeatable formats with recurring characters, a learning point (counting, colours, morals), bright recognisable visuals and plain keyword titles such as 'nursery rhymes' and 'kids songs'. (AIR, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Single language giants still reach the top 15 (El Reino Infantil in Spanish, Luccas Neto in Portuguese, Infobells in Hindi), and Billion Surprise Toys grew through Hindi, English, Indonesian, Korean, Spanish and Arabic versions, showing home market depth and wide dubbing both work. (AIR, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Moonbug's content chief (Jun 2026): stay YouTube first and non exclusive, be on every platform families use, and make content for each viewing moment, from short phone clips on a car journey to longer living room co viewing; hits then extend into Spotify music, toys and live tours. (Senal News, Jun 2026, high)
  • Moonbug gives a whole year a theme (Blippi's 'Year of Vehicles' in 2026) and spins side characters into their own shows (Emmie's Wonder Wardrobe from Little Angel, for ages 3 to 5), a model for giving each Bro Bros friend a themed month or a mini series. (Senal News, Jun 2026, medium)
  • Moonbug says its next growth push in Europe is Spain and France, and its Little Baby Bum brand ('not about music, it is music') moved to a Netflix series with a new curriculum and a soundtrack on every music service, showing music brands travel from YouTube to streaming with audio alongside. (Senal News, 2023 and Jun 2026, medium)
  • Danny Go! (5.43M subs, 6.33B views) earns about 170 to 195M views a month from only 131 videos; its newest upload 'Burning ENERGY! Don't Stop Moving Challenge' (9 Sep 2026) reached about 21.5M views in two months, so a few big, rewatchable movement videos can beat a high upload count. (vidIQ, Nov 2026, high)
  • Ms Rachel had 21.5M YouTube subscribers in Oct 2026 (+1.4% in 30 days) but grew 9.4% on Instagram (6.07M) with about 22 posts a week and 6.7% engagement, showing Instagram can grow fast for a kids brand when parents follow the face behind it. (CreatorDB, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Ms Rachel's most viewed videos are routine and milestone helpers parents search for: a shapes, letters and numbers learning video (287M), potty training (200M), hide and seek with Elmo (152M), friendship and social skills (110M) and a brush your teeth song (106M). (CreatorDB, Oct 2026, high)
  • In vidIQ's US trending channels for the 30 days to 14 Nov 2026, VocaVoca (kids songs) was No. 3 overall with about 1.6B views gained, ahead of CoComelon at No. 17 with about 1.08B, so a Shorts led habit song channel now out gains the biggest brand in the US. (vidIQ, Nov 2026, high)
  • Doctor Sophia, a 'Healthy Habits for Kids' channel built around a doctor character, gained about 651M US views in those 30 days, backing our own best performer (Lion Needs a Doctor) and the case for a recurring friendly doctor or helper in our songs. (vidIQ, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Nursery rhyme channels keep scaling in the US: Lalafun (+654M views in 30 days), Toddler Zoo (+530M) and NuNu TV (+447M), alongside the bilingual Spanish English family music act 123 Andres (+868M), so both classic rhyme songs and bilingual music have big US demand. (vidIQ, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Super Simple Songs reached 20 years in 2026 with over 70M subscribers and over 90B views; it began as teachers writing simple songs to help pupils learn English and now has a self funded Toronto studio of 40+ animators, musicians, puppeteers and educators. (blog.youtube, Aug 2026, high)
  • Super Simple Songs now uploads about once a week, alternating a new song with a 'Top 20' themed compilation (counting, circle time, back to school, book songs), and its themed Top 20 mixes earn 1.1 to 3.5M views each, a steady compilation rhythm for a mature channel. (vidanalyze.com, Nov 2026, high)
  • Super Simple's strongest new song of 2026 is an upbeat 'everybody celebrate' party song (6M views in under three months), ahead of a counting to 20 sea animals song made with another kids channel (3.1M) and a pack your bag trip song (2.7M): party, counting and adventure themes lead. (vidanalyze.com, Nov 2026, high)
  • Lalafun (15.2M subs, 714 videos since Oct 2022) gains 600 to 700M views and 500 to 700K subscribers a month from about 15 uploads a month; its all time hits are classic rhymes with a twist: animal version Wheels on the Bus (955M), Five Little Ducks colours (726M), Old MacDonald safari (407M), counting 10 pandas (337M). (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, high)
  • Lalafun runs local language channels (German, Indonesian, Arabic, Canada and US feeds), and its newest US video uses a mystery question title ('Where did the animals' colours go?'), the same curiosity hook that works for Shorts. (vidiq.com / socialblade.com, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Super Simple Songs also partners with outside brands and channels for single songs (a hockey song with the NHL, a counting song with Noodle and Pals), a way big kids channels borrow each other's audiences. (vidanalyze.com, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Because classroom viewing runs through teachers, songs that work for circle time (a clear learning point, a calm teacher friendly title, no ads style hype) can reach children through lesson plans; Super Simple Songs was built exactly this way by teachers. (chalkbeat.org / blog.youtube, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Bebefinn, Pinkfong's 3D baby and family song brand launched only in 2022, reported more than 80M subscribers and 58B views across its YouTube channels in Jun 2026 and about 84M and 65B by Aug 2026, the fastest climb of any new 3D kids song brand. (thepinkfongcompany.com / khan.co.kr, Aug 2026, medium)
  • Pinkfong's first Amazon Kids+ original, The Big Book of Bebefinn (announced Jun 2026), is 18 musical episodes of about seven minutes following a toddler through everyday firsts such as riding a tricycle, visiting the doctor and trying new foods, mixing new songs with fan favourite hits. (thepinkfongcompany.com, Jun 2026, high)
  • La Granja de Zenon started in 2011 as preschool farm songs, and its named animal characters (a cow, a little rooster, a horse) grew bigger than the songs, so a small cast of recurring named animal friends around the Bros can become the brand kids ask for. (publimetro.com.mx, Sep 2026, medium)
  • When CoComelon's main channel matured it grew through side character spin off channels: JJ's Animal Time reached 200M+ views a month (with its own animal songs and adventures spin offs), Cody Time 60M+ and Nina's Familia 40M+, so an animals themed Bro Bros playlist or sub series is the proven next step once the main channel has traction. (thekidsstreamersphere.substack.com, Oct 2024, medium)
  • Little Angel's main English channel (7.3M subs, 2,278 videos) gained only 0 to 10K subscribers and 14 to 36M views in most months of 2026 (Aug spiked to 125M), so even a Moonbug brand's single channel plateaus without new formats; its Spanish, Portuguese and Indonesian channels carry much of the growth. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, high)
  • 123 Andres, a Grammy and Latin Grammy winning bilingual Spanish English family music act, has 13.2M subscribers and 26.9B views from 3,013 videos, and its monthly views climbed from about 448M (Aug 2026) to 583M (Sep) and 797M (Oct), with new uploads reaching about 800K views in a day and a half. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, high)
  • In vidIQ's US ranking (Nov 2026) Toys and Colors has 123B lifetime views on 83M subscribers (about 1,480 views per subscriber) versus about 1,130 for CoComelon, and Filaretiki has about 1,220, so the channels kids replay most are simple, repeatable play formats rather than the biggest subscriber counts. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Pinkfong and Dr Seuss Enterprises made their first crossover on 16 Oct 2026: a Baby Shark Halloween remix plus a 'Guess Baby Shark's Halloween Costume!' sing along where viewers guess each costume, posted on both channels so each brand feeds the other's audience. (advanced-television.com, Oct 2026, high)
  • Dr Seuss Enterprises' relaunched YouTube channel reached 230.9M views and 2M subscribers in just over two years by turning classic characters into baby versions for nursery style songs, the same baby character trick CoComelon and Bebefinn use. (advanced-television.com, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Disney bought Gracie's Corner, a family made YouTube sing along brand (6.3M subscribers, nearly 10B views) with original songs like a Good Morning Song, Veggie Dance and Play Outside, as a library of 120+ shorts and 18 themed compilations for Disney+ and Disney Jr. from 15 Jun 2026, with new originals to follow. (press.disneyplus.com, 2026, high)
  • Disney Jr. itself makes short stop motion sing along music videos for each holiday (Mickey and Minnie's Holiday Songs: Halloween, then Christmas), so seasonal short sing along clips are a format even the biggest studio uses for preschoolers. (thewaltdisneycompany.com, Aug 2025, high)
  • Peppa Pig revealed in 2026 that little brother George is moderately deaf (new season from 23 Mar, with short clips first on its YouTube channel), its second disabled character after wheelchair user Mandy Mouse, made with the National Deaf Children's Society and a deaf script consultant; disability is still rare in kids TV, so a natural inclusive friend in Bro Bros would stand out. (enablingdevices.com, 2026, medium)
  • LooLoo Kids (62.7M subscribers, 43B views) titles almost every new upload as a medley of its recurring Johny character plus a classic rhyme ('Bath Song + Wheels on the Bus'), and its seasonal Halloween medleys reached 8 to 12M views within weeks in Oct 2026, so naming two familiar songs in one title widens Search reach. (vidanalyze.com, Nov 2026, high)
  • LooLoo Kids' recent uploads cover healthy habits and safety (brushing teeth, bath time, handwashing, bus safety rules) alongside colours, fruits and phonics, showing routine songs are a core, steady earner for top nursery channels. (vidanalyze.com, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Hey Bear Sensory has about 3.4B views from only 95 videos (about 35M per video) with dancing fruit and veggie characters on plain black backgrounds and wordless funk, house and disco tracks; its biggest hits are 10 to 27 minute mixes (Smoothie Mix 383M, Fruit Salad Dance Party 334M), while its recent 1 to 5 minute uploads average only about 5M. (nexlev.io, Nov 2026, high)
  • CreatorDB's study of 417 surging channels over 500K subscribers (Sep 2026) found 24% post 60+ times a month and 42% at least daily, but median subscribers gained per upload were about 98,800 for channels posting under 8 times a month, 16,100 for regular, 6,100 for heavy and only 1,900 for the heaviest posters: volume buys reach, not loyalty. (creatordb.app, Sep 2026, medium)
  • CoComelon's Netflix viewing fell 30% in H2 2025 and the brand moves to Disney+ in January 2027, while creator led YouTube shows (Ms Rachel, CrunchLabs) rose, so the market now rewards a warm personality and fresh feel over a polished nursery rhyme factory. (worldscreen.com, 2026, high)
  • Little Baby Bum (41.6M subscribers, 44.8B views) has had flat or falling subscribers since early 2025 yet still earns about 110 to 140M views a month, proof that an old nursery catalogue lives on recommendations and repeat viewing, not new subscribers. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, high)
  • All five of Little Baby Bum's most viewed videos are Wheels on the Bus variants: Halloween Wheels on the Bus (275M), Ten Little Buses (112M and 69M), Bus Wash (60M) and Wheels on the Bus Underwater (56M), so a single proven song re set in new places and seasons is a repeatable hit engine. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, high)
  • Little Baby Bum's newest Shorts are counting songs with a twist on a known rhyme ('Ten Shiny Stars Roll Out of Bed', 5 Nov 2026), reaching about 53K views in under two days, so classic counting down structures (ten in the bed, five little ducks) adapt well to Shorts. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, medium)

  • GoBooBoo (4.29M subscribers) gets about 36M views a month from only 197 videos, roughly 180K monthly views per video, and its all time hit Baby Finger Where Are You passed 930M views, so a small catalogue of well made songs can outearn a huge one. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, high)
  • Junior Squad has 1,164 videos and 5.45M subscribers but only about 4.4M views in the last 28 days, and its newest 2 minute rhyme got about 3K views in two days, a warning that high volume rhyme uploads without strong recurring characters stall. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, medium)
  • GoBooBoo titles pair an original named song with a known rhyme hook (Boo Boo Song plus Wheels on the Ambulance, 150M) and its second biggest hit is a gentle fear song, Something in the Dark (194M), so mild spooky or brave in the dark themes work for preschoolers. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Stomp! Stomp! Rhinos!, a kids feelings channel that teaches calming down and mindfulness through rhythm, stomping and play, reached 12M+ views in 195 countries, 101K watch hours and 9,800 subscribers within 12 months from 300+ videos and Shorts, so big views can come with few subscribers on kids channels. (studiocity.com, 2026, medium)
  • The Kiboomers (Canada, 4.04M subscribers) earns a steady 50 to 55M views a month while posting only one new video each Friday, and vidIQ models about 37K USD a month, far more per view than mass upload rhyme channels, because its catalogue is evergreen circle time music teachers replay. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, high)
  • All five of The Kiboomers' most viewed videos are movement games: Going on a Dragon Hunt (37.4M), Freeze Dance Song 2 (31.1M), Red Light Freeze Dance (30M), Going on a Dinosaur Hunt (23.4M) and Floor is Lava 2 Freeze Dance (18.2M), and sequels of a winning game (Freeze Dance 2, Floor is Lava 2) do as well as originals. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, high)

  • Kaboochi (2.17M subscribers, 2,225 videos) gets about 11.6M views a month, and its biggest hits are theme twists on the Finger Family song (Football Finger Family 29M, Planet Finger Family 17M), so a known song frame plus a new theme is its main growth trick, though sheer volume gives low views per video. (senalnews.com, Jun 2026, medium)
  • VocaVoca's last 28 days to 12 Nov 2026 brought about 1.42B views versus 2.91B in October, so even a fast rising song channel roughly halved its monthly reach once the Halloween spike passed; seasonal surges must be followed at once by the next season's songs. (vidIQ, Nov 2026, high)

(Vidanalyze, Oct 2026, medium)

  • Lalafun's top recent breakout 'Catch the Black Sheep! Baa Baa Song' reached 33M views in about two months, 30 times its median, and its other hits follow the same title shape: an urgent mission for the baby ('Baby Helps the Itsy Bitsy Spider', 'Paramedic Cures the Boo Boos'), then a well known rhyme anchor, one or two emojis and the channel tag. (OutlierKit, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Lalafun has only 77 videos but 158M views, uploads about three long videos a week averaging nearly 18 minutes with no Shorts, and its estimated audience is the US 44%, UK 17%, Brazil 17%, Turkey 11% and Poland 11%. (OutlierKit, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Super Simple Songs' best recent upload is a remake of its own 'The Animals On The Farm' (38M views, 5.7 times median), and it now publishes 'High Contrast Kids Video' versions of songs (Fruit Is Yummy, 11M), so remaking our own proven hits and making high contrast baby editions are both proven moves.   • Super Simple Songs still uploads only about once a week, recent uploads average about 30 minutes, long form gives 96% of its views, and its estimated audience is the US 53%, UK 18%, Canada 12%, Japan 12% and Vietnam 6%, showing English songs travel to ESL markets in Asia. (OutlierKit, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Kids Diana Show's recent breakouts are scale challenges ('10 vs 100 vs 1,000,000 House Decorating', 43M) and opposite pairs ('Ice Princess vs Fire Princess', 57M) plus story compilations, formats an animated channel can copy as '1 snowman vs 100 snowmen vs 1,000,000 snowmen' style songs. (OutlierKit, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Big kids channels keep about 85 to 90% evergreen content and only 10 to 15% trend based (Lalafun and Kids Diana 90/10, Super Simple Songs 85/15), a useful split for planning our 14 songs a week: roughly 12 evergreen and 2 trend songs. (OutlierKit, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Cocomelon's Instagram (about 790K followers, 1.1K posts) runs as a parenting humour and toddler life account aimed at adults, averaging about 214K Reel plays but only 0.26% likes per follower and about 10 posts in two weeks, far smaller than its YouTube reach. (IQFluence, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Rhymington Square (Canada, 311K subscribers) has 291M views from only 74 videos, about 3.9M per video, built on original friendly monster songs about toddler life; few, strong, rewatchable songs can out earn a big volume of weaker ones. (OutlierKit, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Rhymington Square's recent breakouts are everyday toddler moments told as songs ('It's Way Too Hot Today' 17M, 'Let's Blow A Bubble' 16M, 'My Glasses' 10M) and numbered sequels of proven hits ('There's A Monster In My Tummy #2', 'Open Shut Them #2'). (OutlierKit, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Seasonal holiday songs are Rhymington Square's largest topic cluster (15 of 74 videos) and about 24% of its content is trend based, with audience in the US 61%, Canada 21%, UK 12% and Australia 6%, so holiday songs suit North American audiences especially. (OutlierKit, Sep 2026, medium)
  • TinkaBop Baby Sensory, a young baby sensory music channel, had 11 videos and about 5K subscribers by Sep 2026 with a gentle bath time song as its best (18K views), showing how slowly a new routine song channel grows and how bath, wake up and bedtime routines lead in the baby niche. (OutlierKit, Sep 2026, low)
  • OutlierKit flags dependence on YouTube Kids distribution and on one signature voice as key risks for small baby music channels, so Bro Bros should keep the Bros' voices consistent but build reach on main YouTube, Shorts and Instagram too. (OutlierKit, Sep 2026, low)

  • Cocomelon still uploads about three videos a week averaging about 5.5 minutes with almost no Shorts, keeps about 95% evergreen content, and roughly 10% of recent uploads are Hello Kitty and Sanrio collaborations, showing big brands now team up with other kids IP for fresh reach. (OutlierKit, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Cocomelon's estimated audience is the US 50%, India 17%, UK 17%, Canada 11% and Australia 6%, so India is now as big as the UK for English nursery songs. (OutlierKit, Oct 2026, low)
  • Ms Rachel uploads only about 1.4 videos a month but each runs about 45 minutes, and her top titles lead with a parent search promise ('Baby Learning with Ms Rachel', 'Learn to Talk', 'First Words', '2 Year Old Milestones') before 'Nursery Rhymes & Kids Songs', proof that parents search by developmental goal. (OutlierKit, Sep 2026, medium)
  • An exploratory academic study of English and Portuguese AI made kids videos (2022 to 2024) found music and stories are the main content types, most such videos get low view counts with a few viral exceptions, and many sit on a spectrum of partial AI use; a Bro Bros edge is visible human craft (story, choreography, edits) on top of AI tools. (AoIR Selected Papers (Mintz and Bozzi), Jan 2026, medium)

  • AIR's order of fixes for a stalled kids channel is trust signals first (clean thumbnails, remove weak old videos, resolve yellow icons), then dubbed audio tracks, then 24/7 streams, because languages and streams only work once YouTube trusts the channel. (AIR Media-Tech, 2026, medium)
