  • VocaVoca (US nursery songs) was No. 11 worldwide with about 675M weekly views in late October but missing from the top 50 by the week of 8 Nov, showing how fast weekly views swing for song channels and why we judge our results on monthly, not weekly, trends. (Tubefilter, Oct and Nov 2026, medium)
  • Nigeria's House of Ajebo runs Jeni and Keni, an animated YouTube series about two siblings for ages 1 to 6 with Afrobeat style songs that teach kindness and share African culture (relaunched 2025); sibling led song brands are being built in Africa too, and an Afrobeat flavoured Bro Bros song would speak to West Africa and its diaspora. (BusinessDay Nigeria, Aug 2025, medium)
  • Heidi dan Zidane's Spanish dubbed audience came mostly from Mexico (24.2% of views) and Argentina (11.1%), with Spain watching longest (5:08 average), yet the US gave only 5.3% of views but 43.2% of revenue: volume comes from Latin America, money from US viewers. (AIR Media-Tech, Mar 2026, medium)
  • AIR's Sep 2026 data found AI only dubbed tracks held viewers 4 to 10 times less than pro dubbed tracks on the same videos, with the biggest gaps on kids channels (an Italian kids channel kept 5 to 6 minutes on pro tracks against 54 seconds on its English AI track). (AIR, Sep 2026, medium)
  • On kids channels the voice is part of the product, and an AI voice that sounds too adult, flat or emotionally off can hurt retention and even draw misleading content flags, so Bro Bros dubs should be sung by real or carefully cast child like voices, not YouTube's default auto dub. (AIR, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Good human dubs can match or beat the original: a kids channel with an Indonesian original (165M views) held 2:11 on its pro Spanish track against 2:00 on the original, and Turkish, Italian and German tracks stayed close. (AIR, Sep 2026, medium)
  • On one kids channel with the same videos dubbed three ways, Arabic brought the most views (3.4M) but German, with only 134K views, had the highest share of returning viewers (about 52%) and the longest watch time, so small markets can be the most loyal. (AIR, Apr 2026, medium)
  • AIR judges a new dubbed language by the share of its views that come from casual plus regular (returning) viewers: under 20% after four months is a problem, 20 to 40% is building, over 40% is working and over 60% is strong, and the first 90 days are too early to judge. (AIR, Apr 2026, medium)
  • CIMM's July 2026 report says US young children's video time is now about 35% streaming, 32% YouTube and 16% short form (TikTok, Shorts), with linear TV down to about 6% from 80 to 90% in 2005, and children make up nearly a quarter of all YouTube viewing. (CIMM, Jul 2026, high)
  • US children aged 0 to 8 now average about 2 hours 27 minutes of screen time a day (up from 1 hour 44 in 2005), with mobile the main device, while YouTube Analytics only shows demographics for viewers 13 and over, so our own analytics can never show the true age of our audience. (CIMM, Jul 2026, high)
  • Kenya's Akili TV added read along captions to existing cartoons in a 'Read Along Hour' (Swahili and French pilots) and child viewing rose about 15% while adult co viewing rose about 39%, so big on screen karaoke style lyrics in Bro Bros songs can draw parents in and help reading. (Pulse, Apr 2026, medium)
  • At least 14 kids and family channels sit in France's top 100 YouTube channels by subscribers (11 Nov 2026), led by Masha et Michka (12.2M), Kidi Fun (11.9M), Titounis comptines (7.7M), Kidibli (7.5M) and Little Angel's French channel (5.7M), so French is a strong second language market for nursery songs. (vidIQ, Nov 2026, high)
  • International song brands run separate French channels that rank high in France (Little Angel French 5.7M, HeyKids French 4.9M and its English channel also in France's top 100 at 4.8M), showing French parents subscribe to both local language and English kids song channels. (vidIQ, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Kaboochi's French channel (197K subscribers, 618 videos) earned only about 135K views in the 28 days to 29 Oct 2026 after its uploads stopped in September, with Shorts as its best videos, so a dubbed language channel fades fast without steady uploads; audio tracks on the main channel are safer for a small team. (vidIQ, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Spotify Wrapped 2025 coverage from Nigeria, Ghana, Kenya and South Africa says children now shape whole household listening there, with Cocomelon nursery rhymes, Baby Shark and gentle singalongs like You Are My Sunshine topping family recaps, used for mornings, car rides and bedtime and spread through siblings and cousins sharing one phone. (NewsTrends Kenya, Dec 2025, low)
  • In an AIR test where one kids channel ran the same videos, thumbnails and schedule in Arabic, French and German (16 Feb to 24 Mar 2026), Arabic drew by far the most views (3.4M in five weeks against 745K French and 134K German), confirming Arabic as a high volume first dub for kids songs. (AIR Media-Tech, Apr 2026, medium)
  • In the same test German viewers were the most loyal (about 52% returning, 10.6% regular, new viewers averaging 4:30 against 2:49 for Arabic), so smaller rich markets can repay a dub through watch time and higher ad rates even with fewer views. (AIR Media-Tech, Apr 2026, medium)
  • AIR suggests judging each dubbed audio track by returning viewers, not views: in YouTube Analytics' 'Audience by watch behaviour' filtered by audio track, casual plus regular viewers above 40% means the language is working, 20 to 40% is building and below 20% after four months needs fixing; this share should climb about 3 to 5 points a month in the first year. (AIR Media-Tech, Apr 2026, medium)
  • On one entertainment channel the Spanish dubbed track's viewers watched about two minutes longer per view than the English original (8:55 against 6:59), which AIR puts down to less competition in Spanish. (AIR Media-Tech, Apr 2026, low)
  • Audiences in Russia, Germany, Italy, Spain, Mexico, Brazil and France prefer dubbing, while adults in China, South Korea, India and Japan prefer subtitles; young children everywhere need dubbing because they cannot read subtitles fast enough, so for kids songs only re sung audio tracks count as localisation. (Kapwing compilation (Morning Consult), Apr 2026, medium)
  • Only about 13% of creators in AIR's 3,000 channel network have paid for professional dubbing and 36% have tried AI dubbing, yet dubbed tracks averaged about 45% more views and YouTube says creators with dubbed tracks get over 25% of watch time from non primary languages, so early dubbing is still a gap Bro Bros can use. (Kapwing compilation, Apr 2026, medium)
  • One small counter example: a creator's dedicated Spanish channel got 3,897 views in 90 days against just 32 for the same video's Spanish track on his main English channel, so a separate language channel may beat audio tracks when the main channel has little audience in that language. (Kapwing, Apr 2026, low)
  • In Latin America children aged 3 to 5 use only YouTube for screen content, subscription apps appear around age 6 and games around 9 to 12, so a Spanish and Portuguese dub of our preschool songs reaches the youngest kids on the one screen they all share. (marketing4ecommerce (Kidscorp study), Jul 2026, high)
  • Kidscorp finds YouTube is the only screen with similar reach across high, middle and low income homes in Latin America, reaching about 68% of under 18s in Mexico and Chile versus under 30% for TV, and above 70% digital adoption in Brazil and Argentina. (marketing4ecommerce (Kidscorp study), Jul 2026, high)
  • Across 16 MENA markets kids animation is 40.6% of supply but only 30.2% of demand, while US and Japanese titles drive over 85% of animation demand, so a new kids channel there competes in a crowded field and must stand out with quality and Arabic audio. (Parrot Analytics, 2026 Q1, medium)
  • Children aged 0 to 14 are about a quarter of MENA's population, and parents stay closely involved in judging whether content fits family values, so modest outfits, kind behaviour and no romance matter for Arabic speaking families. (Campaign Middle East, Jun 2026, medium)
  • Pan Arab kids hits mix dialects (the Mansour reboot blends Emirati, Saudi, Iraqi, Egyptian and other voices) and the original Mansour drew most of its 2.5B views from outside the UAE, notably Iraq and Egypt, so an Arabic dub should use simple, widely understood Arabic rather than one local dialect. (Kidscreen, 2023, medium)
  • Sanrio launched 'Sanrio Kids - Cartoons & Kids Songs' on YouTube on 15 Sep 2026 as a global preschool channel whose 3D videos carry Japanese and English audio switchable within the same video, the same multi audio track route open to Bro Bros. (Sanrio, Sep 2026, high)
  • Sanrio Kids splits its channel into three series (sing along music, movement and play including calming down, and everyday story episodes), each about two minutes, a clean playlist structure for a song brand entering Japan and Asia. (Sanrio, Sep 2026, medium)
  • In a Philippine survey of 1,000 parents (29 Oct to 2 Nov 2026), 34% named child specific apps such as YouTube Kids as a safety measure and 97% wanted evidence based rules for children's social media, so Filipino families value clearly kid safe channels. (Daily Tribune Philippines, Nov 2026, medium)
  • The same survey found 60% of Filipino parents say their children use social apps for rest and relaxation and 70% for learning, which favours calm songs and learning songs in Filipino focused playlists. (Daily Tribune Philippines, Nov 2026, low)
  • Vietnam's first music video to pass 1B YouTube views (June 2024) is the kids song 'Mot con vit' about a mother duck and five yellow ducklings, sung by a three year old, from a channel with only about 40 short songs under five minutes; other kids songs hold several of Vietnam's next biggest totals (crocodile song 665M), so a simple animal family song can top a whole country's music charts. (Tuoi Tre, Jun 2024, high)
  • Vietnamese parents use the duck song as a mealtime helper to get children to eat, the same 'song for a daily routine' role our habit songs aim for, so mealtime songs are a strong fit for Vietnam and other Southeast Asian homes. (Tuoi Tre, Jun 2024, medium)
  • POPS Kids, Vietnam's largest kids channel, has about 20.3M subscribers and 15B views from 4,687 videos (Oct 2026), mixing its own shows with licensed Doraemon and Pokemon cartoons, and drew about 3.5M views on a single day; Social Blade puts its income anywhere from about 94K to 7.2M USD a year, showing how wide and unreliable such estimates are. (CafeF, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Nigerian YouTube watch time grew over 50% in a year and views for one network's Nigerian creators rose from 70M to over 400M, yet Nigerian ads pay only about 0.5 to 2.5 USD per 1,000 views against up to 15 USD in the US, so for Bro Bros, Nigeria and other English speaking African homes are reach and word of mouth markets, not revenue markets. (TechCabal, Oct 2025, medium)
  • Germany's 'Kinderlieder zum Mitsingen und Bewegen' channel has 3.47M subscribers, about 33 to 43M views a month from 1,453 videos and vidIQ models about 14K USD a month; it wraps new titles in 'NEU * ... * NEU', its newest song (13 Nov 2026) teaches tying a bow, and its top videos are remixes of singing games such as 'who stole the cookie from the jar' (24.4M) and a dog's lost bone song (22.9M). (vidIQ, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Japan's most viewed kids songs on YouTube are franchise themes (a monster catching game theme at 105M), Japanese language versions of Disney songs and songs from the public TV show 'Okaasan to Issho' (a clock song, a bean hero song), so Japanese children mostly watch Japanese language content and an English Bro Bros song has little pull there until it is re sung in Japanese. (Soundcharts, Nov 2026, medium)
  • German and Japanese hits both lean on practical everyday skills and objects (tying a bow, telling the time), which suggests life skill songs translate across cultures better than culture specific holidays. (vidIQ and Soundcharts, Nov 2026, low)
YouTube rules & safety · 132
  • YouTube's quality principles push high-quality made-for-kids videos up in recommendations and decide entry to the YouTube Kids app. (YouTube Help / YouTube blog, high)
  • Six low-quality types to avoid: heavily promotional, negative behaviour, fake-educational, hard to follow/mass-produced, sensational or keyword-stuffed, odd use of familiar characters. (AIR summary of YouTube policy, high)
  • Red flags: flashing lights, loud or sudden sounds, chaotic camera moves, rushed storytelling, mean characters even as jokes. (Whizzy Studios 2026, medium)
  • YouTube is cracking down on low-effort AI 'slop' for kids; originality and consistent quality matter more in 2026. (AIR Jan 2026 / YouTube CEO letter, high)
  • Getting into the YouTube Kids app is done through review (often via an official YouTube partner), not by applying directly. (AIR, medium)
  • There is no public banned word list, but words in six risk clusters (fear, conflict, medical, danger, emotional overload like 'shocking', adult themes like 'crush') lower kids recommendations and ads. (AIR Media-Tech, Oct 2026, high)
  • Kids and teens has the lowest median RPM of 13 niches, about 0.33 USD per 1,000 views versus about 2.30 overall (300 channels, May 2025 to May 2026), so kids channels win on volume and long watch sessions. (AIR Media-Tech, Oct 2026, high)
  • YouTube requires disclosing AI generated music (its detector does not scan soundtracks, so it is on us); clearly animated or unreal visuals need no label, and for animated work the label sits in the expanded description and does not cut reach. (AIR Media-Tech, Sep 2026, high)
  • Since 27 May 2026 YouTube auto applies AI labels to undeclared photorealistic AI; repeated failure to disclose can lead to removal or Partner Program suspension. (AIR Media-Tech, Sep 2026, high)
  • YouTube's Jul 2026 inauthentic content update names three non monetisable types: generic or repetitive template series with the same situations and outcomes, unsatisfying shock or stitched AI clips, and AI 'experts' on sensitive topics; a clear original creative spin keeps AI assisted work safe. (Tubefilter, Jul 2026, high)
  • The FTC's amended COPPA Rule compliance date was 22 Apr 2026: every video needs an audience setting, YouTube's classifier can mark videos as for kids (appeal only), and Disney paid 10M USD partly for labelling whole channels instead of each video. (PPC Land, Apr 2026, high)
  • Made for kids channels are excluded from YouTube Shopping affiliates and lose personalised ads, comments and notifications, so revenue depends on views and watch time volume. (PPC Land, Apr 2026, high)
  • Suno songs can only be monetised if made on a paid plan (Pro or Premier) at the time of creation; fully AI songs get no copyright, so adding our own written lyrics and arrangement edits protects the human parts. Keep receipts and creation dates per song. (Dynamoi, 2026, medium)
  • Suno settled with Warner Music and is moving to licensed models with monthly download caps; current v5 models will be phased out, so back up the full song catalogue now. (Dynamoi, Mar 2026, medium)
  • YouTube Kids entry runs through automated filters that read thumbnails, titles and the video itself, plus human spot checks and parent reports; paid placements, purchase pushing and toy packaging videos are excluded. (Team YouTube FAQ via PPC Land, Apr 2026, high)
  • YouTube's Mar 2026 Dublin summit treats kids and teens as separate audiences and signals that YouTube Kids will favour less overstimulating, authentic, human made content over mindless AI output. (AIR Media-Tech, Mar 2026, medium)
  • Songs released as YouTube Music Art Tracks through a distributor earn much more per 1,000 streams than Content ID claims (UK 18.87 USD, Germany 14.05, US 11.22), a second income stream for our Suno songs made on the paid plan. (Dynamoi royalty data, Nov 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's five high quality traits for kids content are kindness and healthy habits, curiosity and learning, creativity and imaginative play, life skills with clear lessons, and diverse people and places; videos with them are promoted in recommendations. (YouTube Help, 2026, high)
  • Quality is judged at two levels: a single low quality video gets limited or no ads, while a channel that focuses on low quality content can be suspended from the Partner Program. (YouTube Help, 2026, high)
  • 'Deceptively educational' is a named low quality type: a title or thumbnail that promises learning must deliver accurate learning in the video, so 'learn colours' songs must really teach colours. (YouTube Help, 2026, high)
  • 'Hard to follow' storylines count as low quality, so each song should tell one clear, simple story from start to finish. (YouTube Help, 2026, high)
  • Even large kids channels (PlayToys, 8M subs) can be missing from the YouTube Kids app; after a partner escalated to YouTube's review team on 20 May, the Kids team approved them by 18 Jun, after which they became searchable and appeared as suggestions. (AIR Media-Tech, Aug 2025, medium)
  • YouTube Kids filtering (Team YouTube FAQ, 1 Apr 2026) works in layers: automated filters read thumbnails, titles and the video itself, human reviewers spot check and refine the filters, and parent flags are reviewed around the clock and removed for everyone if upheld. (PPC Land, Apr 2026, high)
  • YouTube Kids excludes paid product placements, videos that push purchases, packaging and unboxing focused videos and excessive accumulation or consumption of products, so our songs should never feature branded toys or buying. (PPC Land, Apr 2026, high)
  • YouTube renamed the Partner Program's 'repetitious content' rule to 'mass produced content' on 15 Jul 2025; with 14 songs a week we must keep each song clearly different in story, setting and moves. (SmartScope, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Community Guidelines strikes: a first violation is usually a warning that expires after 90 days with policy training; three strikes within 90 days can terminate the channel. (SmartScope, Nov 2026, high)
  • YouTube Kids added over 100 Sesame Street episodes in Jan 2026, so trusted educational brands are now direct competitors on the YouTube Kids learning shelf. (PPC Land, Apr 2026, medium)
  • 94% of teachers worldwide use YouTube in class (YouTube, Jan 2026), so classroom friendly songs (brain breaks, routines, counting) can earn repeat plays on school screens. (YouTube Official Blog, Jan 2026, medium)
  • YouTube Music started labelling songs 'created primarily using generative AI' on 14 Oct 2026 (composition and vocals both AI) using partner data and its own detection, with the main YouTube app to follow; per YouTube's May 2026 policy a label alone does not change recommendations or monetisation. (PPC Land, Oct 2026, high)
  • Unlike YouTube, Spotify keeps AI badged artist profiles out of recommendations by default and Deezer removes AI detected tracks from algorithmic and editorial playlists, so AI made songs earn mostly on YouTube, not audio stores. (PPC Land, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Suno songs are commercially usable (YouTube monetisation, distribution) only if made while on a paid Pro or Premier plan; free tier songs never convert, and the status stays after cancelling. (Dynamoi, Oct 2026, high)
  • After the Nov 2025 Warner Music deal, Suno is moving to licensed models and capping downloads (about 20 a month on Pro, 60 on Premier), so we should download and back up every final song and stem as we make it. (Dynamoi, Oct 2026, medium)
  • The EU AI Act's transparency duties (Article 50) applied from 2 Aug 2026, with fines up to 15M EUR or 3% of turnover, adding another reason to disclose AI music clearly in descriptions. (PPC Land, Oct 2026, medium)
  • YouTube confirmed (10 Aug 2026) that from 1 Feb 2027 new Partner Program applicants need 1,000 subscribers plus 8,000 public watch hours in 365 days, or 20M Shorts views in 90 days (double today); members admitted before then are grandfathered, so qualifying before February matters. (PPC Land, Aug 2026, high)
  • From 1 Feb 2027 Shorts pool revenue becomes a rolling test: a channel needs 10M qualified Shorts views in each rolling 90 days or its Shorts pool pay pauses that month; long form ads are unaffected, and partners must accept new modules by 31 Jan 2027. (PPC Land, Aug 2026, high)
  • A channel must also stay active to keep YPP from 2027: 1,000 watch hours in 365 days, 1M Shorts views in 90 days, or at least two long videos or five Shorts every 90 days. (PPC Land, Aug 2026, high)
  • The UK announced (15 Jun 2026) an under 16 social media ban modelled on Australia's (live since 10 Dec 2025), naming YouTube among covered platforms but leaving YouTube Kids outside it; rules are due by end 2026 and should apply from spring 2027. (PPC Land, Jun 2026, high)
  • YouTube Kids is a filtered copy of YouTube: automated filters check each video's thumbnail, title and content and any one can block it, with humans only spot checking; videos built around product packaging, urging kids to buy, paid placements or excessive consumption are kept out, so Bro Bros songs should never show branded toys or 'get yours' moments. (ppc.land (Team YouTube FAQ), Apr 2026, high)
  • Since May 2026 YouTube shows a prominent AI label (under the player, or over a Short) only for photorealistic AI; for animated or stylised content like ours the disclosure appears only in the expanded description, and YouTube says a disclosure label does not change recommendations or monetisation, so ticking the AI box costs us nothing. (musicbusinessworldwide.com, May 2026, high)
  • In AIR's 300 channel study (May 2025 to May 2026) kids and teens content was monetised on only about 32% of playbacks, the lowest of 13 niches, because made for kids videos cannot carry personalised ads, but it gets about a 22% lift in Q4; volume and reach, not rate per view, drive kids channel income. (air.io, Jul 2026, medium)
  • On 15 Oct 2026 YouTube announced an invite only Monthly Shorts Bonus pilot for Partner Program creators in the US, UK, Germany, Brazil, Japan and South Korea; India is not in the first wave, another reason to build US and UK viewers. (air.io, Oct 2026, medium)
  • From 26 Oct 2026 YouTube tests a 20 USD payout threshold (down from 100) for a random group of creators in the US, India and Indonesia, so small new channels may get first earnings sooner. (air.io, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Since 29 Oct 2026 YouTube tests offering some channels with spam policy violations a 30 day cleanup lock (edit or delete videos, or wipe all videos but keep subscribers and handle) instead of termination; repetitive or templated uploads remain the main risk for high volume channels like ours. (air.io, Oct 2026, medium)
  • Seasonal ad money: YouTube RPM runs about 0.5 times its spring baseline in January and about 1.8 to 2.5 times in December, and the two weeks before 25 Dec have the most competitive ad auctions, so our best holiday compilations should be live by the first days of December. (Fluxnote, 2026, medium)
  • Precisify (Feb 2026): 80% of parents say their child asked for something seen in a YouTube ad while co viewing and 50% bought because of YouTube ads, which is why advertisers pay for kids views; our own videos must still never urge purchases, or they drop out of YouTube Kids. (Tubefilter, Feb 2026, medium)
  • Under COPPA and GDPR K, kids content must never ask children to send names, photos, videos or contest entries, nor use sign up links or tracking pixels, and that applies off YouTube too, so our Instagram captions should address parents and never invite kids to submit anything. (AIR, 2026, high)
  • Since Aug 2025 YouTube's AI age estimation can treat signed in viewers it infers are minors as minors even on videos not marked made for kids, changing what is recommended and how ads run; COPPA 2.0 (protections to age 16) is still only proposed in the US. (AIR, 2026, medium)
  • In January 2026 YouTube terminated 16 mass produced AI channels with 35M subscribers and 4.7B lifetime views under its inauthentic content policy; enforcement judges a pattern across a channel's recent uploads (about the last 30), so one batch of templated songs can cut monetisation on every video. (The Next Web, Jun 2026, high)
  • After that purge faceless channels that never used AI also report demonetisation, while niche educational channels held up better; YouTube says AI labelled videos are not penalised and only mass produced, templated content without human creative input is targeted, so visible human craft (our own lyrics, stories, edits) is the protection. (The Next Web, Jun 2026, medium)
  • A Kapwing test found about 21% of the first 500 videos recommended to a new account were AI slop, a New York Times check found over 40% of Shorts recommended after popular preschool videos were AI made, and 230 experts asked YouTube in April 2026 to ban AI content from YouTube Kids, so stricter AI rules for kids are likely. (The Next Web, Jun 2026, medium)
  • India's DPDP rules define a child as anyone under 18, require verifiable parental consent before processing a child's data and ban tracking, behavioural monitoring and targeted ads aimed at children, with fines up to 200 crore rupees; our Indian audience reach must stay on platform made for kids settings, never our own data collection. (KS&K, 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's more prominent AI labels (May 2026) are not shown on YouTube Kids at all, which runs under separate policies, and animated AI videos on main YouTube only get a label tucked in the description; advocacy groups (Fairplay) now ask YouTube to stop recommending AI made for kids videos, so expect future limits aimed at animated AI kids content. (Playtech and Fairplay, May 2026, medium)
  • Neal Mohan's 2026 letter said YouTube would adapt its anti spam and anti clickbait systems to spot and demote low effort AI videos and strictly enforce synthetic media labels, so the defence for an AI assisted channel is clear human authorship and variety, not hiding AI use. (Digit, Jan 2026, high)
  • Since 10 Dec 2025 Australian under 16s are signed out of YouTube: they can still watch, but lose subscriptions, playlists, likes and wellbeing defaults, and parents lose supervised accounts; YouTube Kids is not affected, so in Australia (and later the UK and other copycat bans) the Kids app and signed out recommendations carry family viewing. (blog.google, Dec 2025, high)
  • The EU's Digital Services Act guidelines on minors (Jul 2025) ask every platform kids may use to offer feeds children can reset and understand, private defaults, no overuse features like streaks or endless scroll, and clearly labelled child suitable ads; countdown timers and buy now prompts aimed at kids are discouraged, so our songs must never push kids to buy or demand things. (cadeproject.org, Oct 2025, high)
  • 2026 estimates put Shorts RPM at about 0.10 to 0.32 USD per 1,000 views in the US and 0.09 to 0.19 in Australia, the UK, Germany and Canada, against 0.01 to 0.03 in India, Indonesia and the Philippines, so Shorts views from high income English markets earn about ten times more. (air.io, Jul 2026, high)
  • The UK's first national screen guidance for under 5s (27 Mar 2026) says under 2s should use screens only for shared bonding activities, 2 to 5s at most an hour a day and none at meals or in the hour before bed, with slow paced content and no fast social media style videos; watching together is better than solo use. (ihv.org.uk, Mar 2026, high)
  • Suno's 2026 rights terms (after its Nov 2025 Warner settlement): songs made on a free account can never be used commercially, and subscribing later gives no retroactive licence; songs made while subscribed carry commercial rights for streaming, downloads and sync with no revenue share, but users are no longer treated as owners of the songs. (musicinafrica.net, Dec 2025, high)
  • Suno v6 (9 Sep 2026) runs on models co developed with Warner Music, BMG and Believe and all older models are being retired; free songs can no longer be downloaded and paid plans have monthly download limits (extra downloads cost more), so 14 songs a week plus remixes must fit our plan's download allowance. (thenextweb.com, Sep 2026, high)
  • A German court ruled in 2026, in a case brought by the collecting society GEMA, that Suno breached copyright, and Universal is not part of Suno's licensing deals, so prompts and lyrics must never name real artists or imitate known songs, keeping our catalogue clean if rules tighten. (thenextweb.com, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Since mid 2025 Instagram treats adult managed accounts that mainly feature children with strict message settings, Hidden Words comment filtering, and no recommendations to or from potentially suspicious adults (also harder to find in Search); Meta has not said whether animated child characters count, so a small cut in odd adult reach is possible but parents still see us. (about.fb.com, Oct 2025, medium)
  • Instagram teen accounts default to a 13+ content level modelled on film ratings (US, UK, Australia and Canada since Oct 2025, India since 9 Apr 2026), with an even stricter Limited Content option; accounts flagged for age inappropriate posts lose teen followers and reach, so clean, gentle songs keep our Reels open to older siblings. (ppc.land, Apr 2026, high)
  • Because children under 13 cannot hold Instagram accounts, our Instagram audience is parents, carers and teens; captions and Reels should be written to parents (what the song helps with, a question for them) rather than to the child. (about.fb.com, Jul 2025, medium)
  • vidIQ models only about 10K USD of ad income for Tunes Kids' roughly 30M monthly Arabic views, a sign that Arabic made for kids views earn far less per view than English ones, so Arabic tracks are mainly for reach and brand growth. (vidiq.com, Nov 2026, low)
  • YouTube Help lists what made for kids videos lose on their watch pages: autoplay on Home, cards and end screens, the video watermark (subscribe button), memberships, comments, donate and Super Chat, live chat, merch and tickets, notifications, personalised ads, Miniplayer playback, and save to playlist or Watch Later. (support.google.com, 2026, high)
  • Made for kids channels also cannot use community posts, so channel news (new song days, holiday mixes) can only reach parents through Instagram, video titles and the channel banner, not YouTube posts. (support.google.com, 2026, high)
  • Ads shown in YouTube Kids need pre approval from YouTube's policy team and food and drink, beauty and fitness, dating and political ads are barred, so YouTube Kids views earn less per view than main YouTube and should be judged by reach, not income. (ppc.land (Team YouTube FAQ), Apr 2026, medium)
  • Since 21 Oct 2026 YouTube automatically pauses features on channels that look hacked while it reviews them, usually within 24 hours and without touching monetisation, and since 29 Oct some channels facing spam termination get a 30 day lock to fix videos or appeal instead of instant removal. (air.io, Nov 2026, high)
  • Vietnam's National Assembly debated in 2026 restricting minors' use of some social apps and stricter identity checks for under 16s, citing Australia's ban (Dec 2025) and similar steps in Indonesia and France since Mar 2026; nothing is law yet, but signed out family viewing on TV and YouTube Kids is where Southeast Asian kids markets are heading. (vietnamnews.vn, 2026, medium)
  • Shorts that use licensed music from YouTube's library are capped at 60 seconds, but our own original Suno songs are not, though 25 to 40 seconds remains the performance sweet spot. (toptal.com, 2026, medium)
  • More than 200 child advocacy groups and child development experts (2026) asked YouTube to ban AI generated videos from the YouTube Kids app and give parents tools to limit AI content on main YouTube; for an AI assisted channel like ours, proof of human craft (original songs, designed characters, real story and learning goals) is the best protection if rules tighten. (emarketer.com, 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's synthetic content disclosure rule exempts clearly unrealistic animation, so our 3D cartoons do not need an 'altered or synthetic' label, while anything that looks realistic (photoreal people, fake real world footage) would; scripts and captions written with AI also need no disclosure. (wired.com, Mar 2024, high)
  • A Los Angeles jury found Google and Meta negligent in designing YouTube and Instagram and failing to warn users (25 Mar 2026, 6M USD, appeal planned), a bellwether for thousands of child harm cases with more trials due in 2026, so expect further kid safeguards such as less autoplay and scrolling that reduce passive binge views. (thestar.com.my (Reuters), Mar 2026, high)
  • With pressure on attention grabbing design for kids, songs that end calmly, invite a real world action (go brush your teeth, time for bed) and avoid endless cliffhangers fit where regulators and parents are pushing YouTube, and are less likely to be hit by future limits.   • The same guide says kids content runs at roughly half the normal CPM (made for kids ads are not personalised) and that Q4 and Ramadan bring 30 to 50% RPM spikes in the Middle East, so Arabic songs should be ready before Ramadan 2027 (about mid February). (fluxnote.io, Mar 2026, low)
  • AIR's 274 channel RPM study (Sep 2026) found Kids and Teens Shorts had a 45.7% monetised playback rate against only 12.1% for long form because of made for kids ad limits, about 2,156 Shorts views equal 1,000 long views for large kids channels, and Shorts made 41% of revenue for big kids channels posting 21+ Shorts a month. (air.io, Sep 2026, medium)
  • In the same study a kids channel's long form RPM fell about 26% in months when it posted Shorts, and Shorts RPM by viewer country was 0.33 USD in the US against about 0.16 to 0.19 in Australia, the UK, Canada and Germany (correlational, vendor data). (air.io, Sep 2026, low)
  • Since March 2026 YouTube has been testing a mobile pop up that asks viewers to rate on a five point scale whether a video feels like AI slop, and it now labels AI made videos automatically from C2PA metadata and SynthID watermarks rather than relying only on creator disclosure, so visible human craft (story, gags, varied scenes) protects AI assisted songs like ours. (thenextweb.com, Jun 2026, medium)
  • A 7.55M subscriber kids channel cut long uploads from 21 to 11 and raised Shorts from 10 to 23 in Q4 2025 against Q3, moved to 4K with TV safe text, higher contrast colours and audio mixed for TV speakers, and revenue grew about 34% on roughly 6% more views. (air.io, Apr 2026, medium)
  • FluxNote's unsourced 2026 seasonal model puts November RPM around 3 times January and December about 3.6 to 5 times January, with the two weeks before 25 December the most competitive ad auctions of the year, so our best Christmas songs should be live by late November.   • The New York Times' Feb 2026 test found the AI slop served to toddlers had warped faces, garbled text, no repetition, no story and no clip longer than 30 seconds; YouTube removed five cited channels from the Partner Program and YouTube Kids, so Bro Bros Shorts should always show a repeated hook and a tiny beginning, middle and end. (NYT via Digital Trends, Feb 2026, high)
  • YouTube divides Shorts pool money with music rights holders by the number of licensed tracks used (one track takes roughly half of that Short's share), so Bro Bros Shorts built only on our own original songs keep the full creator share, a quiet money advantage over channels using library music. (AIR Media-Tech, Aug 2026, medium)
  • Since March 2026 videos that show young subjects in distress with shocking imagery lose ad eligibility, so no crying or scared baby faces in Bro Bros thumbnails or opening frames, even for 'feelings' songs; show the happy ending face instead. (AIR Media-Tech, Aug 2026, medium)
  • YouTube Premium Lite (no ads on most videos) is expanding to all Premium countries but keeps ads on music content and excludes YouTube Music, so song videos still earn ad money from Lite subscribers where other videos do not. (AIR Media-Tech, Aug 2026, low)
  • Content ID also scans multi language audio tracks, and YouTube skips auto dubbing on any video with a copyright claim, one more reason every Bro Bros track and dub uses only our own Suno music. (AIR, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Sydney the Song Cat, a hand drawn kids song channel made over four years, was demonetised as 'inauthentic content' in spring 2026 even with a clean record, because it reused rigs and backgrounds; appeals with storyboards and voice recordings got template replies and it had to wait for a July re apply window. (Cartoon Brew via Pulse, May 2026, medium)
  • In February 2026, shortly after YouTube removed big AI slop channels, popular kaiju and dinosaur animation channels also lost monetisation with no clear guidance, so reused 3D assets plus repetitive templates can be read as mass produced even when the work is original. (Creator Handbook, Feb 2026, medium)
  • To protect monetisation every Bro Bros song should leave a visible human trail: an original story idea per video, varied scenes and choreography, real lyric changes rather than template swaps, and saved making of material (Suno prompts, Meshy prompts, Blender files, storyboards) to attach to any appeal. (Cartoon Brew and Creator Handbook, 2026, medium)
  • The FTC's amended COPPA Rule (in force June 2025) had a compliance date of 22 April 2026, and YouTube used that date to require every creator, even non kids ones, to set the audience per channel or per video; FTC civil penalties reach about 53K USD per violation. (PPC Land, Apr 2026, high)
  • Once YouTube's systems apply a made for kids label the creator cannot change it, only appeal in Studio, and made for kids channels are excluded from the YouTube Shopping affiliate programme, so merchandise links must live off YouTube (website, Instagram). (PPC Land, Apr 2026, high)
  • Meta announced in June 2026 that its 13+ content setting for teen accounts (no suggestive, graphic, strong language, risky stunt or alcohol content in Explore, Reels and search) goes global across Instagram, Facebook and Messenger after India got it in April; clean family songs are not affected, but risky stunt jokes would be. (MakeInfluence, 2026, medium)
  • The Elf on the Shelf is a trademarked character and brand, so Bro Bros must design its own helper elf (different name, look and story) and never use that name or look in titles, tags or thumbnails. (Romper, 2024 (brand check), medium)
  • Much Brazilian funk and phonk carries explicit lyrics and adult dance styles, so Bro Bros borrows only the rhythm (a bouncy tamborzao style drum pattern) in its own Suno songs, never the trending tracks or their moves. (Billboard Brasil, Jul 2026 (our judgement), medium)
  • CIMM notes that kids content earns lower CPMs, that ad tech often cannot work in COPPA compliant settings so children's videos are left out of programmatic buying, and that advertisers rarely buy YouTube made for kids inventory, which is why kids channel RPMs stay low and scale matters. (CIMM, Jul 2026, high)
  • Since May 2026 YouTube auto detects and labels undisclosed photorealistic AI video, placing the label under the player on long videos and as an overlay on Shorts, but animated or stylised content (including animated AI music videos) only carries a disclosure in the expanded description. (Music Business Worldwide, May 2026, high)
  • YouTube says an AI disclosure label does not change recommendations or monetisation eligibility, and creators can correct a wrong label in Studio except for permanent labels on videos made with YouTube's own AI tools or carrying C2PA fully AI metadata. (Music Business Worldwide, May 2026, high)
  • Brazil's ECA Digital (child and teen online safety statute) has been fully in force since 17 Mar 2026 for services likely to be used by minors, including streaming and social platforms and foreign providers: it bans ad profiling of minors and compulsive design features and requires protective defaults and guardian linked accounts under 16. (Mayer Brown, Apr 2026, high)
  • Brazil's regulator scheduled administrative sanctions under ECA Digital from November 2026 and formal enforcement from January 2027, with fines up to 10% of Brazilian revenue; its decree on child influencers needs court approval to monetise real children, which does not touch an animated channel like ours. (Mayer Brown, Apr 2026, medium)
  • Because laws in Brazil, the EU and the UK now target designs that encourage compulsive use, Bro Bros should avoid manipulative hooks aimed at kids (fake countdowns to keep watching, 'don't stop now' prompts) and keep endings that invite a calm goodbye. (Mayer Brown and earlier rules leaves, 2026, medium)
  • The Nutcracker story and Tchaikovsky's music are public domain, but specific film or ballet company designs are not, so Bro Bros can use a toy soldier and music box idea with our own designs and our own newly written melody rather than any recording. (general copyright note (our judgement), medium)
  • Creator data compiled in 2026 puts long form RPM highest in Australia (about 10 to 12 USD per 1,000 views), the US (9 to 11), Canada (8 to 10) and New Zealand (8 to 9), and lowest in Bangladesh, Pakistan and the Philippines (about 0.2 to 0.5) and Vietnam (0.3 to 0.7). (videodubbing.com, Aug 2026, low)
  • The same compilation puts the kids and animation niche at about 1 to 4 USD RPM (median about 2.5) for US heavy long form audiences, with roughly a 1.5 times multiplier in Q4. (videodubbing.com, Aug 2026, low)
  • For US facing channels December RPM averages about 74% above January lows, while lower paying markets see only about a 25 to 30% Q4 lift, so our December holiday songs aimed at the US, UK, Canada and Australia are the year's best earning uploads and January needs cheaper, evergreen production. (videodubbing.com, Aug 2026, low)
  • Trends that put a real child's photo into a frame (for example Santa's nice list) are popular with parents, but Bro Bros must never ask viewers to send children's photos or names, keeping to made for kids privacy rules. (ViralRemix, Nov 2026 (our rule), high)
  • YouTube renamed 'repetitious content' to 'inauthentic content' in July 2025, and creator guides report its checks look for upload cadence high for the channel's age, audio that clusters tightly from the same AI tool and presets, near identical thumbnails, formulaic titles and low watch time, so at 14 songs a week Bro Bros must vary Suno style prompts, instruments and tempos song to song. (OutlierKit, 2026, medium)
  • Reused content enforcement on AI audio is reported to hit three patterns: the same AI beat under different thumbnails, re uploads of others' AI tracks, and long compilations that loop one short segment with small changes, so our Friday compilations must be made of distinct full songs, never one song looped. (OutlierKit, 2026, medium)
  • AI music is not banned from monetisation: creator guides say disclosure through the 'Altered or synthetic content' setting does not by itself disqualify a video, while raw unedited AI dumps and voice clones of real artists are what gets demonetised; checking each track with YouTube Studio's copyright check before publishing and regenerating any claimed track is advised because AI models can echo songs from their training data. (OutlierKit, 2026, medium)
  • Under Indonesia's PP Tunas child protection regulation, Google committed in a 17 Apr 2026 letter to a minimum YouTube account age of 16 there, began removing under 16 accounts gradually and said it would stop ads targeted at children, after the ministry reprimanded YouTube on 10 Apr and listed it among eight 'high risk' platforms. (Jakarta Post, Apr 2026, high)
  • Because Indonesian children can no longer hold their own YouTube accounts, our Indonesian audience will mostly arrive through parents' accounts, signed out viewing or family devices, so Indonesian titles, descriptions and dubbed tracks should speak to parents' searches (lagu anak, lagu tidur) rather than to kids typing. (Jakarta Post, Apr 2026, medium)
  • As of Aug 2026 COPPA 2.0 had passed the US Senate unanimously and the KIDS Act passed the House (29 Jun 2026), but neither was law, and KOSA was still awaiting a committee markup, so the amended COPPA Rule (compliance from 22 Apr 2026) remains the binding US standard for our videos. (Mayer Brown, Aug 2026, high)
  • Four US states have app store accountability acts (Texas in force after a 4 Jun 2026 court stay, Alabama from 1 Jan 2027), 26 states have age verification laws and California may now require age estimation, so more platforms will know a viewer is a child and route them to kid settings, which rewards correctly marked made for kids videos. (Mayer Brown, Aug 2026, medium)
  • The EU Kids Act, proposed on 17 Sep 2026 and still needing Parliament and Council approval, would ban personal accounts for under 13s on platforms including YouTube, allow parent set 'mini accounts' for ages 13 to 15 with up to one hour a day, and end self declared birthdates. (Euronews, Sep 2026, high)
  • The same proposal would switch off engagement based recommendations for minors by default, ban behaviour profiled feeds for them and prohibit endless autoplay and infinite scrolling, so in Europe our growth would lean on search, subscriptions, playlists and parents' own feeds rather than kid personalised recommendations. (Euronews, Sep 2026, medium)
  • Australia's eSafety evaluation (data from March 2026) found the share of under 16s with a social media account fell only from 52.4% to 42.1% after the ban, mostly because age checks were weak, and most families said they were not yet affected. (eSafety Commissioner, 2026, medium)
  • AIR's September 2026 study puts Shorts RPM by audience country at about $0.33 for the US, $0.19 Australia and about $0.16 to $0.17 for the UK, Canada and Germany, so a million Shorts views earn about $300 from a US heavy audience but only $150 to $250 from a mixed global one. (AIR Media-Tech, Sep 2026, medium)
  • The same study found channels with audiences mainly in Nigeria, Pakistan or the Philippines see long form CPMs of only $0.20 to $1.50, against $8 to $20 for European, American or Australian audiences, so dubs for lower paying markets grow reach while English keeps the revenue. (AIR Media-Tech, Sep 2026, medium)
  • YouTube's Q3 2026 ad revenue was about $11.2B, up 9.2% year on year, the slowest growth of 2026 (11% in Q1, 13% in Q2), so ad rates are not rising fast and Bro Bros revenue growth has to come mostly from more watch time, especially long TV views. (PPC Land, Oct 2026, high)
  • Nielsen put YouTube at a record 15.8% of all US TV viewing in August 2026, and viewers watch about 2B hours of Shorts a month on TV screens, which keeps living room friendly long compilations the most valuable format for a kids song channel. (PPC Land, Oct 2026, high)
  • Premium connected TV CPMs reportedly jumped from about $34 to $43 during the US midterm political ad season (to early Nov 2026), and that demand ends after election day, so late November and December prices will depend on holiday retail advertisers rather than politics. (PPC Land (Digiday), Oct 2026, low)
  • Mexico's online child safety decree took effect on 14 Oct 2026 and requires covered platforms (social networks, games, app stores) to verify users' ages without relying only on self declared age, with compliance reports due 13 Nov 2026, so Mexican kids may increasingly watch through parent or supervised accounts. (Sheppard Mullin, Oct 2026, medium)
  • New York's final SAFE for Kids Act rules (released 28 Jul 2026, in force 25 Jan 2027) forbid giving minors an algorithmic 'addictive' feed without age assurance or verified parental consent and ban notifications to minors between midnight and 6 AM, while still letting minors see content without the feed, so search, playlists and subscriptions matter more for reaching older kids. (ZwillGen, Aug 2026, medium)
  • The Q4 ad boost is smaller for kids content: AIR data summarised by Gyre (Nov 2026) shows median RPM rising about 22% in Q4 for kids and teens versus about 60% for business and finance and 39% for food, because made for kids videos only carry contextual ads. (Gyre, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Across niches, regular video RPM peaked in November 2025 (median 2.35 USD, 2.12 in December, 1.56 in January) while live stream RPM peaked a month later in December (5.47 USD versus 2.31 in January), so a Bro Bros 24/7 holiday stream started before Black Friday (27 Nov 2026) catches the best paying weeks. (Gyre, Nov 2026, medium)
  • For an unattended 24/7 stream YouTube serves no mid roll ads in Manual mode, so mid rolls must be set to Automatic, and the stream must use only music we own (Content ID claims interrupt it) and real moving content rather than a still image or slideshow, which can break monetisation rules. (Gyre, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Malaysia began enforcing a ban on social media accounts for under 16s on 1 Jun 2026 for platforms with at least 8M users, naming YouTube along with Facebook, Instagram and TikTok; existing accounts are age checked over six months, so Malaysian kids will mostly watch Bro Bros signed out or through parents' and supervised accounts, not their own. (AP via WTOP, Jun 2026, high)
  • Ofcom found 73% of UK 11 to 17 year olds met harmful content in a four week window (36% of them on YouTube) and asked platforms for safer feeds, but YouTube declined major recommendation changes (May 2026), so UK made for kids discovery rules are unchanged for now; fines can reach 10% of global revenue if Ofcom acts. (Shopifreaks, May 2026, medium)
  • India's DPDP Rules (notified Nov 2025) roll out in stages: consent manager registration from Nov 2026 and most other duties by May 2027, with Rule 10 letting platforms check parental consent through age or identity tokens from authorised entities such as DigiLocker, so Indian children's accounts and comments will narrow further next year. (ThePrint, Nov 2025, medium)
  • Since late May 2026 YouTube shows AI labels as an overlay on Shorts and directly under the player on long videos, but non realistic content such as animation and fantasy is excluded from mandatory disclosure, so our stylised 3D cartoon songs do not need the realistic AI label, while any photoreal scene or voice clone of a real person would. (PPC Land, May 2026, high)
  • The House passed KIDS Act (H.R. 7757, 29 Jun 2026) bundles 14 proposals: it would extend children's privacy protection to teens, ban targeted ads to minors, make the most protective settings the default and restrict autoplay, push notifications and infinite scroll for minors; if it became law, autoplay chains would weaken and each Bro Bros video would need to earn its own click from Home and Search. (Crowell, Jul 2026, medium)
  • The KIDS Act drops KOSA's duty of care, and Senate KOSA sponsor Richard Blumenthal called the House version dead in the Senate, so no new US federal kids online law is likely before 2027 and the current COPPA rules remain the ones we follow. (Crowell, Jul 2026, medium)
  • Texas's App Store Accountability Act has been enforceable since 28 May 2026 while its appeal runs (the Fifth Circuit let it stand on 5 Jun), while Utah's deadline moved to 6 May 2027 and Louisiana's to 1 Jul 2027; app stores pass apps age bands (under 13, 13 to 15, 16 to 17, 18+) and parental consent signals, so more US children will reach YouTube through supervised and kids profiles. (Wiley, 2026, medium)
Our channel results · 58
  • YouTube channel stands at about 2,140 subscribers with 450 to 1,400 views a day in 1 to 15 Nov 2026; the best days (10 and 12 Nov) followed the Jungle Doctors song and a Hindi Didi story. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • Top long form uploads 1 to 15 Nov: Lion Needs a Doctor (833 views), Light the Diyas (783), Ek Diya Sabke Liye (456), Stomp Your Boot hoedown (382), Dhol Bajao (356), Who Took the Cookie (319): story songs with a problem to fix and festival songs lead. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • Average view duration on our long form songs is only about 15 to 25 seconds; cartoon episodes hold a little longer (up to 36 s for Buddy's Birthday) but get fewer views, so early retention is our biggest weakness. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • Our YouTube Shorts get almost no views (1 to 30 each) while the same moments on Instagram Reels get 50 to 125, so YouTube is not yet testing our Shorts with seed audiences. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • Instagram Reels lose 58 to 90% of viewers in the first 3 seconds; the lowest skip rates were the Wave Jump Hop action song (58%), the Sports Day race (59%) and a Hindi Didi cloud story (59%), the highest were Diwali Dhamaka (90%) and Buddy's Birthday episode (88%). (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • Likes, comments and shares are near zero on both platforms (best: 3 shares for Jalao Deep on Instagram), so watch and skip behaviour is the only real signal we get. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • Reel views were about 100 to 125 each in the first week of November but mostly 20 to 90 after 9 Nov, so Instagram reach is softening and the hook needs to improve. (Metricool, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Given our Shorts get almost no YouTube views, the data points to fewer, better Shorts cut fresh from each song's most energetic moment, rather than more volume. (Metricool Nov 2026 compared with AIR cases, low)
  • Metricool's per video YouTube data runs about 4 days behind (on 15 Nov the newest row was the 11 Nov upload), so judge each song only after 4 or 5 days, not on its first day. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • Even for an Indian festival, the English titled Diwali song Light the Diyas reached 783 views while the Hindi titled Jalao Deep rock song reached only 69, so English titles reach a wider audience for us. (Metricool, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Our songs outperform our cartoon episodes about 3 to 1 on views (songs mostly 100 to 830, episodes mostly 10 to 220) with similar 15 to 36 second average watch, so songs should stay the core output. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • The 10 AM IST song uploads hold most of our top results (Lion Needs a Doctor, Light the Diyas, Ek Diya, Stomp Your Boot, Dhol Bajao), while 4 PM slots, often episodes, trail; content type and slot are mixed so the slot effect is not proven. (Metricool, Nov 2026, low)
  • Simple sing along manner songs did modestly (Thank You 197, Zoo Stomp 102, Birthday Shake 49) compared with songs built around a small problem or mission (Lion doctor 833, Cookie mystery 319, Treasure hunt 251). (Metricool, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Instagram Reels posted 9 to 14 Nov got only 5 to 91 views each, down from about 100 to 125 in early November, so our Instagram reach has kept falling for two weeks. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • The best Reels that week were the mystery (Who Took the Cookie, 91 views), the bedtime shy monster lullaby (65) and the pirate treasure hunt (62): a question or a little mystery in the first line pulls more viewers than an action list. (Metricool, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Average Reel watch time is mostly 2 to 4 seconds; the exceptions were Blast Off space bus (about 14 s), the Hindi grumpy cloud story (about 12 s) and the giant pumpkin garden song (about 9 s), all with a clear transformation (rocket launch, rain, seed to pumpkin). (Metricool, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Saves and shares on our Reels are still zero across the week, so nothing yet makes parents send our Reels to others, the strongest Instagram reach signal. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • About 66% of our YouTube views in 1 to 15 Nov (about 9,200 of 14,000) came from Suggested videos, so being recommended next to other kids songs is our main engine; titles, thumbnails and topics should match the big channels' popular songs. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • YouTube Search brought about 21% of our views (about 3,000) and subscribers only about 7%, so keyword clear titles (animal, doctor, Diwali, story song) already pay off. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • The Shorts feed gave only 37 views and playlists 11 in two weeks, so our Shorts are not being tested in the feed and our playlists are almost unused as a discovery path. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • Our YouTube subscribers are about 65% India and 8% US, then Pakistan (4%), Philippines (2.6%), UK (2.3%) and Bangladesh (1.6%); South Asia dominates while English songs slowly build the US and UK base. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • Our scheduled YouTube posts mark 'AI generated content' as false even though the music is made in Suno; YouTube asks creators to disclose AI made music, so this setting needs a check. (Metricool scheduled posts, Nov 2026, high)
  • Metricool reports our Instagram follower count as about 1 in Nov 2026 (worth checking in the app); if true, so every Reel view comes from non follower recommendations; growing even a few hundred followers (for example by asking parents to follow in captions and bio) would give each Reel a base audience. (Metricool, Nov 2026, medium)

(Metricool, Nov 2026, high)

  • Daily YouTube views sat at about 450 to 750 most days but jumped to 1,386 on 10 Nov and 1,296 on 12 Nov, the Diwali song days, so festival songs posted two to three days ahead roughly double our daily views. (Metricool, Nov 2026, high)
  • Only 2 YouTube shares were recorded across 1 to 14 Nov, so nothing we post is yet being passed between parents; a share worthy hook (a dance challenge or a family moment) is still missing. (Metricool, Nov 2026, medium)

(Metricool planner, Nov 2026, high)

Trends & challenges · 159
  • Trend windows are short: a trending sound can go from fresh to saturated within days (about a 3-5 day window), so trend content must be made and posted fast. (SocialPilot / PostNitro, 2026, medium)
  • Reels using trending audio get about 42% more engagement, but for made-for-kids content we use our own original songs in the trend's style; Reels with original audio that others reuse also get a boost. (Vaizle 2026 / blitzcut, medium)
  • Instagram Reels formats this month (Nov 2026): '5 stages of...' mood arc in five clips, locked-camera outfit transition on one move, timestamp day stories, 'Not every angel' text-joke punchline. (Later Reels roundup, Nov 2026, high)
  • October 2026 Reels formats: '0% to 100% loading' across cuts, 'Recharged' 1% to 100% battery before/after, 'Toggle on' switch reveal, fan transition outfit swaps, 'my reaction' faces only. (Later Reels roundup, Oct 2026, high)
  • TikTok Oct/Nov 2026: 'Ramalama' reveal (camera starts at the feet and tilts up on the beat to reveal a full costume), runway walk recreations, kitchen dance clips. (B&T / Fabulate, Nov 2026, high)
  • The biggest kid-friendly dance trend of 2026 is the 'AI baby dance' / sway dance: a simple side-to-side sway and wave routine, recreated by real families and kids with millions of views. (Know Your Meme / Daily Dot, Jan-Sep 2026, high)
  • Holiday nostalgia dances go viral each December: last year's awkward Charlie Brown-style party dance mashup united families (the characters are protected, but the goofy group-dance idea is not). (NewEngen Jan 2026 / YPulse, medium)
  • Winter-themed simple moves (snowflake arms, spins) became a global challenge in January 2026; easy, repeatable seasonal moves spread fastest. (AcademicJobs news, Jan 2026, low)
  • Easy, low-impact, non-contact moves (whip, sway, wave, clap patterns) are the ones schools and families copy; simplicity is what makes a dance challenge spread. (Dreamina / Nickelodeon trend guides 2026, medium)
  • Food and big machinery topics keep topping YouTube Shorts charts in November 2026; timely, event-like releases beat random uploads. (Tubefilter newsletter / mean.ceo, Nov 2026, medium)
  • A shoulder bounce dembow track ('Dame Hombro', 184K TikTok videos by late Oct 2026) spread from Latin America to Spain and Italy within weeks, used in a playful 'AI version vs handmade version' bounce, so a simple shoulder bounce move on a Latin beat is a cross border dance hook (we use our own beat, never the track). (que.es / socialpilot.co, Oct 2026, medium)
  • The 'I'm so sorry' carousel (four or five photos building up a plan, then the funny real result) is a top early November 2026 TikTok format with kid costume versions, a plan versus reality joke that suits a Bro Bros Short where the brothers' big plan goes comically wrong. (socialpilot.co, Nov 2026, medium)
  • 'Costume loading' (everyday clothes, a loading caption, a freeze frame, then a cut to the full costume on the beat drop) is a top TikTok transition this month; in our 3D scenes the same freeze and cut on the drop can reveal the brothers as astronauts, chefs or dinosaurs. (socialpilot.co, Nov 2026, medium)
  • TikTok feeds swung from Halloween to Christmas decor content on 1 Nov 2026 ('the Christmas switch'), so our holiday Shorts can start now rather than waiting for December. (socialpilot.co, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Later's 13 Nov 2026 roundup lists three new Reels formats: a fixed camera outfit change triggered by one simple move, timestamped clips of a single visit or routine, and the 5 stages of mood arc; all use original audio, so they suit our own songs. (Later, Nov 2026, high)
  • Halloween formats peaked around 1 to 2 Nov (the 'I'm so sorry' costume carousel sound jumped from 174K to 408K videos in six days) and fade fast; SocialPilot notes the format, not the audio, drives most trends and that they often peak within 7 to 10 days, so a trend must be shipped within a week. (SocialPilot and Que.es, Nov 2026, high)

  • A US weekly TikTok tracker (about 15 Nov 2026) counts group dances where two to four friends repeat one easy move at about 29.7M views and short 4 to 8 count routines meant to be learned fast, matching our 'one signature move per song' rule. (howtogoviral.app, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Harmless 'comically tiny version of an everyday item' jokes (a tiny snack, a tiny cup) reached about 17.1M views that week, and escalating jokes where an ordinary task ends hugely exaggerated about 18.2M, both easy silly beats for Bro Bros story songs. (howtogoviral.app, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Beginner tutorials that show three clearly separated moves and then the full routine (about 1.8M views for one dancehall version) are a ready Short structure: teach move one, two, three slowly, then dance it all at full speed. (howtogoviral.app, Nov 2026, medium)
  • A childhood then and now photo carousel set to a slow classic rock song about time passing gathered nearly 9M views in a few weeks (Masterhooks via Fast Company, 10 Nov 2026): slide one shows someone as a small child, slide two shows them now doing the same thing, a format Bro Bros can copy with baby versions of the brothers and our own gentle song. (Fast Company, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Epidemic Sound's 13 Nov 2026 TikTok roundup lists a 'first time in a fancy cafe' gag (someone sits on a strange looking table thinking it is a stool while another person uses it correctly), a kid safe slapstick idea of picking the wrong seat that suits a silly song. (Epidemic Sound, Nov 2026, medium)
  • The 'out of breath' dance challenge has people dance full out to a strong beat until they are exhausted, ending on a dramatic flop or funny face (15 to 60 seconds, top clips past 10M views, parents joining kids), a ready ending for any high energy Bro Bros song. (AcademicJobs, 2026, low)

(Later, Epidemic Sound, inmagic, Nov 2026, medium)

  • A December 2025 TikTok staple was the 'signature line' trend: stare into the camera, wait for the beat, then say the phrase you repeat most, a format that fits each Bro Bros character saying his own catchphrase on the beat in a Short and likely to return this December. (Kapwing, Dec 2025, medium)

(Kapwing, Dec 2025, medium)
  • Frozen bubbles went viral again in February 2026 (a toy brand's TikTok, reviving a 2022 trend): families blow homemade bubbles at or below about minus 7 C and watch feathery ice crystals spread across them, a winter science moment perfect for a gentle Bro Bros winter song visual. (Motherly, Feb 2026, medium)

(Kapwing, Dec 2025, medium)

(What's on Netflix, Nov 2026, medium)

(tryvolume.app, Nov 2026, medium)

  • The 'last uppy' trend, where parents lift their tweens and teens for one final carry hug, began with one father's post in late 2025, has hundreds of thousands of TikTok posts and is still being written about in Nov 2026, so a gentle growing up moment where Big Bro gives Little Bro one more carry fits warm year end and New Year content. (Today's Parent, Nov 2026, medium)

  • On 1 Nov 2026 the top US trending video was a Halloween songs playlist, 6 of the top 20 trending videos were less than 24 hours old, and analysts single out seasonal, mood based music playlists because families leave them running, which supports posting our Christmas and winter compilations by late November. (mean.ceo (YouTube trends), Nov 2026, medium)
  • Early signs for Christmas 2026 toys (9 Nov) favour nostalgia that parents share with kids: a modern remake of a 1990s giggling plush (about 30 USD), a retro electronic pocket pet with a pop culture crossover (about 15 USD, a likely stocking filler) and limited mini collectible playsets that adults grew up with, so 'toy wakes up and giggles' and 'look after your little pocket pet' stories will match what kids unwrap. (ConsumerAffairs, Nov 2026, medium)
  • This week's trend trackers (Later 13 Nov, NewEngen 13 Nov, SocialPilot to 10 Nov, Volume 15 Nov) mostly repeat formats already in our trend bank, and the new holiday audio is all famous copyrighted Christmas songs, so for December we copy formats (reveals, countdowns, trio dances) with our own Suno songs rather than chase any sound. (Later, NewEngen, SocialPilot, Nov 2026, high)
  • Last December kids creators did best when the toy or reveal sat inside a story arc such as a quest to 'save Christmas', or when viewers chose between two paths or two gifts, which kept both children and parents watching; heavy selling made parents switch off. (Influencity, Dec 2025, medium)
  • A classic Christmas cartoon republished by a big studio reached about 12M views within a month in Dec 2025, and creators' 'top 25' holiday lists steer family watchlists, so older seasonal songs come back every December and our winter songs should be refreshed and bundled each year rather than replaced. (Influencity, Dec 2025, medium)

(Daily Hive, Legit.ng, Yahoo, 2025 to 2026, medium)
  • ppl.studio's Nov 2026 TikTok board rates 'pet doing human things' clips as a proven family format (41 creators using it) and lists cute baby moments and 'little me' costume photo carousels as proven too, so a Bro Bros pet character that brushes its teeth or dances like the brothers is a ready made Short. (ppl.studio, Nov 2026, medium)
  • The same board's top dance format is friends doing a trend dance in one continuous living room take (top post about 50.2M views), so a single unbroken wide shot of both Bros and friends dancing on a sofa set can outperform heavily cut dance edits. (ppl.studio, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Rising formats on the board include 6 second 'growth stages' montages (a seed or crop growing in quick steps) and 18 second wordless 'wait for the twist' village gags, both language free formats that suit a worldwide kids audience and our planting and surprise songs. (ppl.studio, Nov 2026, medium)
  • Since July 2026 Gulf brands (Uber UAE, Visit Abu Dhabi, du and a UAE ministry) post Instagram content styled as if a child grabbed the phone: crooked selfies, a finger over the lens, blurry close ups and random captions, which reads as play rather than an ad; a 'Little Bro took over our account' Reel fits our characters perfectly. (Khaleej Times, Jul 2026, medium)
  • Teacher and pupil dance clips are an evergreen viral format across South Africa (a teacher joining two pupils in a trending dance passed 1.5M views in two days, Jan 2026), India and the US, with viewers loving an adult who matches the kids' energy, so an original teacher character who joins the Bros' class dance is a safe, relatable Short. (Briefly News, Jan 2026, medium)

  • GivingTuesday is Tuesday 1 Dec 2026 and is run in more than 110 countries; in the US in 2025 about 38.1M people took part, 13.5M donated goods and 11.1M volunteered, so a 'share one toy' kindness song or Short posted on 1 Dec rides a global generosity day without any religious tie. (GivingTuesday, 2026, high)

Research log

  • 2026-11-16 04:00 Run 299 (rules (US KIDS Act contents, state app store age laws)): The House passed KIDS Act (H.R. 7757, 29 Jun 2026) would extend privacy protection to teens, ban targeted ads to minors, require the most protective default settings and limit autoplay, push notifications and infinite scroll, but the Senate sponsor of KOSA calls it dead there. Texas's app store age law has been enforced since 28 May 2026 during appeal; Utah's deadline moved to 6 May 2027 and Louisiana's to 1 Jul 2027, with stores passing age bands (under 13, 13 to 15, 16 to 17, 18+) to apps. Total 1272.
  • 2026-11-16 03:59 Run 298 (TRENDS (December calendar hooks: GivingTuesday, treat days, sweater day, inclusion day)): GivingTuesday falls on Tue 1 Dec 2026 and runs in more than 110 countries (US 2025: 38.1M people, 13.5M donated goods), a ready hook for a sharing and kindness Short. Other kid friendly December hooks: International Day of Persons with Disabilities (3 Dec), International Volunteer Day (5 Dec), Brownie Day (8 Dec), Gingerbread House Day (12 Dec) and US Ugly Christmas Sweater Day (18 Dec). A kindness chain format joined the trend bank. Total 1269.
  • 2026-11-16 03:58 Run 297 (competitor deep dive (Bebefinn main channel slowdown and newest upload)): Bebefinn's main English channel has 37.3M subscribers and 25.1B views from 1,086 videos, adds about 370M views a month, but its subscriber gains slowed to 100K to 300K a month in 2026 from 500K to 1.3M in 2024 and 2025. Its newest upload (13 Nov, 'We Are Car Mechanics! Let's Fix the Car!') passed 133K views in 13 hours, and its top video is still a 2022 good morning routine song at 775M. The same slowdown pattern seen at BabyBus and Little Angel. Total 1266.
  • 2026-11-16 03:52 Run 296 (global markets (Germany and Japan)): Germany's 'Kinderlieder zum Mitsingen und Bewegen' (3.47M subscribers, about 33 to 43M views a month from 1,453 videos) tags new songs with NEU at both ends of the title, its newest teaches tying a bow, and its biggest hits are remixed singing games like 'who stole the cookie from the jar'. Japan's top kids songs by YouTube views are franchise themes and Japanese versions of Disney songs plus public TV songs, so English originals travel least there and Japanese should be a later dub. Total 1263.
  • 2026-11-16 03:51 Run 295 (TRENDS (kid took over the account, teacher joins the dance, original audio rising)): A Gulf wide Instagram format has brands posting as if a child grabbed the phone (crooked selfies, a finger over the lens, random captions), copied by Uber UAE and Visit Abu Dhabi. Teacher and pupil dance clips keep going viral in South Africa, India and the US (one hit 1.5M views in two days). Tokchart's 15 Nov chart shows the fastest growing TikTok sounds are nearly all creators' original audio. Two formats joined the trend bank. Total 1260.
  • 2026-11-16 03:49 Run 294 (visual craft (baby colour study, emojis in titles, title length and kids naming)): Sussex Baby Lab (Jun 2026, 54 babies, 100 book pages) found infants look longest at high contrast, saturated reds, blues, purples and teals and least at pastels, the opposite of what adults rated best. vidIQ's 128.5M video study found one or two emojis lift Shorts views (median +13%) but not long videos, and AIR's 18,080 channel title study found 30 to 50 characters best, with questions and numbers weak for kids and character first, series style names easier to classify. Total 1257.
  • 2026-11-16 03:49 Run 293 (music craft (Suno solo vocals, songs with staying power)): A Sept 2026 Suno guide shows how to keep one clear lead voice (describe 'one clear lead singer' first, push the chorus lift into instruments, list choir, crowd, gang vocals and harmonies in Exclude, avoid words like anthemic or singalong in Style, fix single sections with Replace Section). Hit Songs Deconstructed's 2025 staying power report found long running hits are shorter (3:17), open faster (11 second intros), use pre choruses far more (73% vs 40%) and borrow 1980s sounds. Playbook rule on vocals updated. Total 1254.
  • 2026-11-16 03:47 Run 292 (TRENDS (pet does human things, growth stages montage, wordless twist gag, one take living room dance)): ppl.studio's weekly TikTok board (Nov 2026) lists a 'pet doing human things' format used by 41 creators, a one take living room friends' dance whose top post has 50.2M views, rising 6 second crop growth stage montages and 18 second wordless 'wait for the twist' gags. All are kid safe and wordless enough to travel; two joined the trend bank. Total 1251.
  • 2026-11-16 03:47 Run 291 (Shorts and Reels (Content ID on long Shorts, clip length habits, trial Reel test rules)): Little new on Reels ranking since August. Practical finds: a Short longer than one minute with an active Content ID claim can be blocked worldwide, so if our songs go to a distributor with Content ID, our own channel must be whitelisted first; OpusClip's 2.9M clips cluster at 30 to 60 seconds (40%) with only 4% under 15 seconds, a habit not proof; Planoly's test rules for Trial Reels are fresh footage, one change at a time and trial versus trial comparisons after 24 hours. Total 1248.
  • 2026-11-16 03:46 Run 290 (YouTube algorithm (original Shorts priority, Shorts off switch, subscriber vs non subscriber learning)): YouTube announced on 1 Oct 2026 that the Shorts feed will favour original content and cut reach for channels that mainly re upload others' clips, judged at channel level and with no notice. Since April 2026 any viewer can set Shorts time to zero, which removes Shorts from Home, Search and Suggested, so Shorts only channels lose those viewers. Todd Beaupre explained that early results are learned separately for subscribers and new viewers and that most subscribed viewing happens in Suggested, not the Subscriptions feed. Total 1245.
